## Fix 14 — serialize team membership, mission and tracking updates

Resumed the existing, uncommitted `fix/wildfire-14-team-serialization` branch, whose HEAD is main **a958a2e77040c80fe77f5ea76a52361cccf13cd2**. Ran `git fetch origin main` and verified FETCH_HEAD equals that exact commit before continuing. Did not build on a Fix 13 branch. Work remains in the authorized work clone; no dependency installation, push, PR or merge.

### Mechanisms and changed files

- **TEAM-3 — `server/atomic.ts`, `server/routes/teams.ts`, `server/fs.ts`:** rotation reads a fresh collection snapshot, then atomically revokes observed codes, sweeps codes dead for seven days, creates the new code, and updates the team's `activeJoinCode` pointer. The shared team-document read/write serializes concurrent rotations in both SDKs. A retried transaction also reads/revokes the pointer's code, even when the caller's collection snapshot preceded its creation. Transaction rejection returns unavailable/HTTP 503 without publishing a replacement or partially revoking existing codes. Missing/inactive team and code collisions fail closed. Fix 10's prefix invalidation is already present in main: `docSet` calls `invalidateCollectionCache`, which deletes all `${collectionName}::` query keys; the original sequential mint invalidates the collection correctly. The fresh rotation read additionally avoids cross-process cache staleness; the pointer closes the concurrent snapshot gap.
- **TEAM-4 — `server/routes/teams.ts`:** check the durable deactivation result; return 503 on failure and retain the live registry. Only successful writes remove the registry entry and return success.
- **TEAM-5 — `server/routes/teams.ts`, `server/atomic.ts`:** capture the mission before validating GPS evidence (also for manual arrivals), pass expected `sosId` and the existing `since` dispatch generation, and compare both inside the transaction before any phase write. A replacement returns `stale-mission`/HTTP 409 `MISSION_CHANGED`; timely requests still succeed. No new mission version scheme.
- **TRG-1 — `server/fs.ts`, `server/teamRegistry.ts`, heartbeat call in `server/routes/teams.ts`:** replace unconditional snapshot merge with a transaction on the member document. Require the member to exist, remain active, retain the same team and token generation. A concurrent removal either purges an earlier snapshot or causes the transaction to retry and skip its GPS write. Heartbeat supplies the generation it validated. Preserve the display payload and existing throttle behavior; TRG-2 remains out of scope.
- **`tests/team-serialization.test.ts`:** sixteen real-function regressions (eight scenarios, each Admin + client SDK), with only the Firestore boundary faked. Real Express router, JWT/admin authorization, cache helpers, transactions and registry run unchanged. The optimistic fake tracks document reads, stages writes, rejects whole commits and retries on changed reads. Deferred gates force both rotation snapshots to precede mint, and force a staged GPS write to resume after the real removal route acknowledges `gpsPurged:true`. Arrival gates replace the mission between validation/read and transaction commit, including manual requests. An SDK import is warmed before concurrent requests to avoid a Vitest lazy-import mock race.
- **`tests/team-registry.test.ts`, `tests/teams-join.test.ts`, `tests/teams-operations.test.ts`:** adapt existing mock names/call contracts to the new atomic helpers and mission-identity argument. Preserve snapshot throttle/display-field assertions. Old mocked sweep call assertions move to actual stored-document assertions in the real-function regression: old revoked/expired codes disappear, freshly revoked codes remain, another team's live code remains live, and this team's previous code is revoked. No behavioral coverage is intentionally removed.
- **`FIX_PLAN.md`:** this mechanism, proof and validation record.

## Fix 14b — invalidate the cached mission after an identity conflict (CodeRabbit Minor 4654202479, review 6007169699, from PR #144)

Fixes the follow-up Minor from PR #144's CI watch: after a stale-mission conflict, the cached mission was not invalidated, so an immediate retry returned `MISSION_CHANGED` again until the 60 s `DOC_CACHE_TTL_MS` expired.

### The defect

The mission-phase route reads the mission via cached `docGet("teamMissions", token.teamId)` (`server/routes/teams.ts:690`). When another server instance replaces the mission, this instance's cache still holds the old identity. The transaction correctly detects the mismatch and returns `"stale-mission"` — but the route's stale-mission branch returned 409 with no invalidation, unlike the success path just below it, which does `invalidateDocCache("teamMissions", token.teamId)`.

### The fix

- `server/routes/teams.ts` (+5 lines): the `"stale-mission"` branch now calls `invalidateDocCache("teamMissions", token.teamId)` before returning the 409, with a Fix 14b comment. The `"no-active-mission"` branch was NOT changed: the same staleness shape does not apply there (a dispatch invalidates the teamMissions cache when a new mission is created, so a retry after a new dispatch reads fresh).

## Fix 15 — roster GET returns 503 on a read failure instead of a successful empty unsaved roster (RSTR-5, Major)

Fixes RSTR-5 (report line 596): GET called `docGet` and serialized `day?.posts || []`, `saved:Boolean(day)`. `docGet` returns null for an actual SDK failure, so the handler could not distinguish a DB outage from a genuinely nonexistent day. An operator shown an empty roster during an outage, then saving that display after recovery, would replace the real day.

### The fix

- `server/routes/roster.ts` (GET `/:date` only): the read now goes through the already-imported `getFreshDocResult` (discriminated `found` / `missing` / `error` — its docstring in `server/atomic.ts:242–254` says it was added specifically to distinguish DB outage from missing staff). `error` → 503 `{ error: "Database not available" }` (same wording as this file's PUT/POST write failures); `missing` → the unchanged 200 `{ posts: [], saved: false }` shape; `found` → the saved day. The GET now reads fresh from the server instead of the 60 s doc cache — appropriate for operator-critical roster data, and PUT already invalidates the doc cache on save.
- `tests/roster.test.ts`: the `atomic.js` mock's `getFreshDocResult` gained a `vi.hoisted` state switch (`missing` / `error` / `found`) plus two new regressions: a failed read returns 503 with no `posts`/`saved` fields, and a found day returns 200 with its posts and `saved: true`. Existing GET tests are unchanged and still pass (missing → empty unsaved shape).

Out of scope (noted, not fixed): the same null-confusion exists in DELETE (`:294`, read failure → 404 "Roster not found") and copy-to (`:333`, read failure → 404 "No roster found to copy from") — but both refuse the operation, so neither can destroy stored state; they are a follow-up, not this finding. RSTR-6/RSTR-7 are Fix 42, untouched. PUT's `existing` read (`:199`) is used only for the post-save diff log, so a failed read there cannot corrupt data — untouched.

### Verification

- Fail-without-fix (parent, replicated independently): the 2 new tests run against the unfixed production code (via `git stash` of `server/routes/roster.ts` only) failed — **2 failed / 21 passed**; the fix was restored afterwards (`git stash pop`, both files modified).

- `npm run build`: pass.
- Affected suite `tests/roster.test.ts`: **23/23 passed** with the fix.

## Fix 15b — a failed roster load must not install an editable empty roster (CodeRabbit Major 4654542820, review 6007631774, from PR #146)

Fixes the follow-up Major from PR #146's CI watch: the server-side 503 from Fix 15 did not close the finding's harm, because `fetchRoster`'s non-OK branch in `src/components/RosterBoard.tsx:97–100` installed `{ unitId: "", date, posts: [], saved: false }` with no error message, the save button stayed enabled (roster non-null, date matching, not loading), and `saveRoster` PUT `{ posts: [] }` — an operator saving that stale board after the DB recovered would replace the stored day with an empty roster.

- `src/components/RosterBoard.tsx` (non-OK branch of `fetchRoster` only): on any non-OK load other than 401 it now calls `setRoster(null)` and sets the localized load-error message (`"تعذر تحميل الجدول."` / `"Erreur de chargement."`, the same wording the `catch` branch already used). The 401 branch is unchanged (it refetches the shared session). The "add post" form still renders while writable, but it POSTs a single post via the atomic append endpoint — an explicit operator action, not a wholesale replace of the day — so it is out of scope for this finding.

Regression: a 503 roster load shows the load error, shows no empty-roster hint, and leaves the save button disabled. Control: a successful load shows the saved post and leaves save enabled.

## Fix 15c — a thrown roster load must also clear the roster (CodeRabbit Major, review 6007713491, from PR #146 re-watch)

## Fix 16 — correct native mesh delivery and SOS encoding (NSVC-1 + NSVC-5, both Major)

Fixes NSVC-1 (report line 1500) and NSVC-5 (report line 1505), both in the native mesh path.

NSVC-1: locally queued plaintext is encrypted separately for each connected peer (send loop), but a receiving peer B stored the received ciphertext/signature as a relay with `needsEncryption` left false and re-emitted that ciphertext verbatim to C. C holds a different private key, so decrypt returns null and the frame is skipped — A→B→C could never deliver A's report text to C, even though B decrypted it successfully. Hop allowance only moved bytes addressed to B.

NSVC-5: `AppRepository.broadcastMeshIntel` selected wire type `echo` for kind `sos`, and `sendSos` uses that path before HTTP. MeshService encrypts EVERY locally queued type with AES-GCM, including echo — but MeshInbound treats echo as plaintext base64 and bypasses decryption, so `notifyListeners` received raw ciphertext decoded as UTF-8 ("encrypted garbage"). AppRepository could not parse that as JSON intel and never admitted the SOS coordinates. This contradicted MeshInbound's comment that no legitimate caller emits echo.

### The fix

NSVC-1 — re-encrypt on relay (`MeshService.kt`, `MeshQueue.kt`):
- `MeshQueue` (companion) gained a pure, JVM-tested relay policy: `RelayMode { VERBATIM, REENCRYPT, NONE }` + `relayMode(type, decrypted, hopsLeft)`. `hopsLeft <= 0` → NONE; `echo` → VERBATIM (plaintext by design, every hop reads it); a decrypted non-echo frame → REENCRYPT; an undecryptable frame → VERBATIM (the intended recipient may be downstream).
- `handleIncomingMessage` now dispatches on that policy instead of always relaying verbatim, still BEFORE the listener notify (audit B1 order preserved) and still under the same queue bound. The verbatim construction moved verbatim into `enqueueVerbatimRelay`.
- `enqueueReencryptedRelay(payload, plaintext)` mirrors `broadcastMessage` exactly: one snapshot for the PoW prefix, the origin identity and the eventual encryption; a fresh PoW solved for (origin messageId, our snapshot id) (difficulty 8, cheap); the same type and coordinates; `hopCount = 0`; one hop consumed (`hopsLeft - 1`); `needsEncryption = true` with the snapshot attached, so the send loop encrypts it separately for each of our peers. The re-encrypted copy is a fresh origin frame under our own key — the origin's signature does not (and cannot) survive a re-encryption, because the ciphertext it covers is replaced. **The copy keeps the origin `messageId`**: both mesh dedup mechanisms (anti-replay seen-cache, Trickle/forwarded-marker suppression) are keyed by it, so a fresh id would make every copy look like a new message and fan out exponentially (~66k frames for one report in a 10-device clique at MAX_HOPS 5). With the origin id, the markers bound re-sends per peer; the nonce stays fresh, so the copy still passes the seen-cache at its first hop.
- Fallbacks preserve today's behavior exactly: an oversized plaintext (> 256 KB, which `broadcastMessage` would reject at queue time) falls back to the verbatim relay; a PoW-budget exhaustion or any exception while building the copy falls back to the verbatim relay. Exactly one copy is admitted per frame — never both.
- Scope note: the re-encrypted copy's frame timestamp is set at send time by `encryptForPeerWithSnapshot` (like every local message), so content can keep gossiping hop by hop while it keeps moving; the hop budget (`MAX_HOPS`), not the clock, bounds its spread. The verbatim relay keeps the origin timestamp. A frame whose bytes are not valid UTF-8 re-queues with the same replacement characters the UI path already produces.
- Follow-up (not this part): the same report can still be NOTIFIED twice when heard over two paths — the seen-cache key covers (messageId, nonce) and the re-encrypted copy's nonce is fresh, so the second copy is accepted and displayed again. Content dedup in `onMeshPlaintext` (skip notify/re-origination for a (type, plaintext, lat, lng) already seen within a window) is a separate change; the wire fan-out is bounded by the kept messageId, but the duplicate display is not fixed here.

NSVC-5 — SOS rides as `report` (`AppRepository.kt`):
- `broadcastMeshIntel` now always sends `"report"` instead of `"echo"` for kind `sos`. SOS is JSON intel like any report: receivers decrypt it through the normal path, `onMeshPlaintext` parses the JSON, and the coordinates are admitted to `meshIntel`. No legitimate caller emits echo anymore; the wire type stays accepted on the receive path for backward compatibility with older builds. SOS stays E2EE (its coordinates are sensitive emergency data) — the alternative of sending echo as true plaintext would have exposed them to any nearby sniffer.

### Verification

- `npm run lint`: 0 diagnostics.
- `npm run build`: pass.
- Full `npm run test:server`: **875/875 passed**.
- Full `npm run test:react`: **1042/1042 passed**.
- `git diff --check`: passed.
- New tests `MeshQueueTest` (4 cases pinning the relay-mode matrix: decrypted report → REENCRYPT, echo even when decrypted → VERBATIM, undecryptable report → VERBATIM, no hops left → NONE). **Kotlin proof is CI-only**: this VM has no Java/Gradle/Android SDK/kotlinc (same as Fix 7), so the new unit tests and the Android build are verified by the PR's CI checks, not locally. There is no local fail-without-fix run for the Kotlin tests for the same reason; the policy function is pure and pinned, and the service wiring (helpers, PoW, snapshot, hop accounting) was verified by reading against `broadcastMessage` / `sendToTarget` / `MeshInbound.evaluate` field by field.
- NSVC-5's one-line type change is not JVM-testable (`AppRepository` needs an Android `Context` and there is no `AppRepositoryTest` harness); its correctness rests on the read-through: `broadcastMessage` accepts `"report"`, the send loop encrypts report frames, receivers decrypt them in `MeshInbound`, and `onMeshPlaintext` parses the JSON and admits coordinates — all pre-existing, covered paths. CI build + existing Android tests are the gate.
- Independent review (reviewer agent, `~/workspace/wildfire-fix-prep/fix16-review.md`): verdict ISSUES on the first draft — 1 Major + 1 Nit. Major: the re-encrypted copy minted a fresh UUID, defeating both messageId-keyed dedup mechanisms and fanning out exponentially (~66k frames for one report in a 10-clique); fixed before merge by keeping the origin `messageId` (see above). Nit: stale "echo" wording in `broadcastMeshIntel`'s comment — reworded. Everything else verified clean (field-by-field NSVC-1 trace, NSVC-5 end-to-end trace, relayMode matrix + tests, scope exactly 5 files, no Kotlin compile risks found by reading).

Deviations: the Xingchi gateway's `/v1/responses` endpoint was still down (sustained 503 since ~18:10 CET 2026-11-21, ~26 hours by this round), so Sol never started; the user explicitly instructed at ~20:15 CET 2026-11-22 to continue without Sol and start Fix 16 now. The parent implemented the fix directly (same pattern as 11b/11c/12b/12c/14b/15/15b/15c) from the findings and the existing code. No scope expansion. Reviewer verification is done (see above); remaining for **PR #147**: push, PR creation, CI watch, merge.

### Fix 16b — move RelayMode out of the companion object (CodeRabbit Critical 4659699286)

CI (build-and-test, release, Analyze java-kotlin) failed on both PR heads with `MeshService.kt:667:13 'when' expression must be exhaustive` + `Unresolved reference 'RelayMode'` at :668/:669/:678. Cause: the enum was declared inside `MeshQueue`'s companion object, so `MeshQueue.RelayMode` does not resolve (it would be `MeshQueue.Companion.RelayMode`); the companion FUNCTION `relayMode` resolves, which is why the compiler could name the missing branches. The parent and the independent reviewer had both read the placement as legal Kotlin — the build log proved otherwise, and the mistake is owned plainly here. Fix: declare `RelayMode` directly inside `MeshQueue` (class body); `relayMode` stays in the companion; all call sites (`MeshQueue.RelayMode.*`, `MeshQueue.relayMode(...)`) unchanged. Also fixed the stale kdoc that still said the re-encrypted copy is queued with a "new id" (the review fix kept the origin id).

### Fix 16c — a readable copy supersedes an unreadable one (CodeRabbit Major 4659699299)

Admission did not dedup by `messageId`, so one device could hold two queued entries for the same messageId: an unreadable verbatim copy (relayed from a peer who couldn't decrypt it) and a readable re-encrypted copy. The forwarded marker is keyed `"$endpointId:$messageId"`, so whichever entry reached a peer first blocked the other for that peer — if the verbatim entry went first, the peer received only ciphertext it cannot read and the readable copy stayed suppressed until the marker expired (5 min) or the peer disconnected. This reintroduces the NSVC-1 harm order-dependently (created by keeping the origin `messageId`). Fix: `MeshQueue.isReadable` (`needsEncryption`, or echo) + `hasReadableEntry` + `dropUnreadableFor` (drops queued unreadable entries for the id AND clears its forwarded markers; the Trickle-K count is derived from the markers each tick, so it resets too). `enqueueVerbatimRelay` skips admission of a non-echo verbatim copy when a readable entry for the same id is already queued; `enqueueReencryptedRelay` drops unreadable entries for the id before admitting the readable copy. Safe: markers for the id can only have been set by the unreadable entries' sends (a readable entry queued earlier would have blocked the unreadable admission, and markers from a readable entry sent >5 min ago have expired); dropping the unreadable copy loses nothing — every peer it could reach is also reachable by the readable copy, which is strictly better. Tests: 4 new `MeshQueueTest` cases (drop scope, marker clearing, no-op return, `hasReadableEntry` matrix). The duplicate-display Minor (4659699293) stays the documented follow-up (content dedup in `onMeshPlaintext`), not fixed here.

### Fix 16d — clear delivery state when a readable copy replaces an unreadable one (CodeRabbit Minor 4659815315)

`dropUnreadableFor` removed the unreadable entries and cleared their forwarded markers, but not `deliveredTargets[messageId]` or the message's `payloadToMessage` bindings. `deliveredTargets` is keyed by `messageId`, shared across copies, and `sweepExpiredMessages` evicts a message early when all its `attemptedTargets` are in it — so the unreadable copy's acknowledgements could evict the readable replacement on the next sweep before its own transfer completed, and if that transfer then failed, no queued entry remained for retry. Fix: inside the `if (removed)` branch, `deliveredTargets.remove(messageId)` + remove `payloadToMessage` bindings for the id (2 lines). Safe: at drop time the replacement has not been admitted yet, so it has no bindings; a late SUCCESS for the dropped copy finds no binding and cannot falsely acknowledge the replacement. Tests: 1 new `MeshQueueTest` case (delivery-state clearing, scoped to the id) + the no-op test extended to assert delivery state is untouched when nothing is dropped.

## Fix 17 — preserve native snapshots across failed, empty and oversized refreshes (NREP-1 + NREP-2 + NREP-3 + NAPI-1, all Major)

Fixes NREP-1, NREP-2, NREP-3 (report lines 1352–1354, all in `AppRepository.kt`) and NAPI-1 (report line 1543, in `ObservatoryApi.kt`). The four compose: NAPI-1 *manufactures* malformed-success bodies (truncation), NREP-2 *fails to recognise* them (empty list instead of failure), NREP-1 *stamps and persists* a partial/mixed refresh as a full success, and NREP-3 *resurrects* the stale snapshot the other three failed to replace. All four land together — any one alone leaves a hole.

NREP-1: `refreshAll` decided "everything worked" with `||` (OR) over reference-inequality tests, so one endpoint succeeding stamped the whole refresh as successful, advanced `lastSyncMs`, and persisted a snapshot mixing fresh data for one list with stale data for the other three — contradicting the function's own kdoc promise ("only a fully-successful pass updates lastSyncMs/persist"). NREP-2: the parsers collapse "malformed body" and "genuinely empty array" into the same `emptyList()`, and `fetchList` returned that list for every HTTP 200, so a malformed 200 (`{}`) wiped last-good data instead of preserving it. NREP-3: `persistSnapshot` returned early whenever reports AND hotspots were both empty, even after an entirely successful refresh established that both are empty — the old snapshot was never replaced or cleared, so the next cold start restored removed/resolved threats (and stale safezones); a safezones-only snapshot was never persisted at all. NAPI-1: `readBody` allocated `MAX_RESPONSE_BYTES + 1` (the `+1` sentinel byte exists precisely to detect overflow) and then never checked it — it decoded only the first 32 KB and handed the truncated prefix back as a successful `Result.Ok`. The server serves full reports/satellite lists with no size ceiling, so any busy period legitimately exceeds 32 KB; truncated JSON fails to parse as an array, so the parsers returned `emptyList()` and (via NREP-2) the app wiped its last-good data.

### The fix

NREP-1 — AND, not OR (`AppRepository.kt`, `RefreshPolicy.kt`): `refreshAll` now keeps the nullable fetch results separate from the `?: prev` fallback and decides success with `RefreshPolicy.refreshSucceeded` (all four non-null). Partial refreshes still *display* the fresh data that did arrive (last-good-correct); only `lastSyncMs` advancing and `persistSnapshot()` are now gated on all four succeeding. The old `!==` identity trick is deleted. The kdoc at ~458 already promised this behaviour and needed no change.

NREP-2 — malformed 200 is a failure (`AppRepository.kt`, `RefreshPolicy.kt`): `fetchList` now checks an empty parse result against `RefreshPolicy.isGenuineEmptyBody` — true only for a body that is exactly `[]` modulo whitespace. Any other body that parses to nothing returns `null` (failure → caller keeps last-good data). A valid array whose every item is malformed is also treated as a failure (zero usable items must not wipe last-good data) — documented as intentional in the code. `Models.kt` is untouched (its full review is pending, and the finding says so). `restoreSnapshot` needs no change: its inputs are always valid arrays (`"[]"` or a real stored array string).

NREP-3 — persist the empty snapshot (`AppRepository.kt`): the early return in `persistSnapshot` is deleted — that is the whole fix. After a fully-successful refresh that establishes zero threats, the written snapshot is `{reports: [], hotspots: [], safezones: […], lastSyncMs: <fresh>}`, replacing the stale record; the safezones-only case is fixed by the same deletion. The other four callers (submit/drain paths) now also write when the lists are empty — harmless, they write the current state (empty arrays + current `lastSyncMs`, possibly 0 on a never-synced device), and `restoreSnapshot` already defaults missing keys to `"[]"` / `0L`. Deleting the stored key instead was considered and rejected: persist-empty keeps `lastSyncMs` and the safezones in one code path.

NAPI-1 — detect overflow, fail loudly, raise the cap (`ObservatoryApi.kt`): `readBody` now returns `String?` — `null` when a 2xx body could not be read completely (overflow or stream error) — and `request` maps that to `Result.TransportFailure` (callers already treat that as "keep last-good / queue and retry"). The bounded read is extracted as the pure, JVM-tested `readBoundedBody(stream, maxBytes)` (`null` = overflow or stream error; a body of exactly `maxBytes` decodes fully; a truncated body is never decoded, so a multi-byte character can never be split). Error bodies keep the pre-Fix-17 behaviour (`readErrorBody`: truncated at the cap, `""` on failure) — the status itself was read successfully, and turning an unreadable error body into a transport failure would flip a known 4xx into the retryable path. `MAX_RESPONSE_BYTES`: `32 * 1024` → `1 * 1024 * 1024` (now `internal` so the test pins it); the class kdoc's false "bounded 32KB reads — every endpoint returns bounded JSON" claim is corrected to the real contract (bounded at 1 MiB, anything larger is a transport failure, never a truncated success). The 1 MiB cap is still a cap — a catastrophic fire season could in principle push a list past it; the outcome is then fail-loud (last-good preserved, retry next poll), never silent corruption. `TeamLocationService.kt` has its own separate 4 KB `readBody` — out of scope for this part, untouched.

### Verification

- `npm run lint`: 0 diagnostics.
- `npm run build`: pass.
- Full `npm run test:server`: **875/875 passed**.
- Full `npm run test:react`: **1041/1042 passed** — the single failure is the known `tests/dataset-outages.test.ts:114` 5 s timeout flake ("returns 503 past the absolute age bound after repeated refresh failures", a Fix 12 SZM-2 test, untouched by this part — Fix 17 changes no TS files). The file passes **17/17 in isolation** (37.56 s — the file is slow and exceeds the per-test timeout only under full-suite load). The same flake hit Fix 14 run 2 once and did not reproduce there either.
- `git diff --check`: passed.
- New tests `RefreshPolicyTest` (7 cases: all-four-succeed incl. genuinely empty lists, exactly-one-failure ×4, all-fail, genuine-empty-array forms, malformed/non-array bodies, non-empty array, fail-without-fix shape) and `ObservatoryApiBodyTest` (6 cases: small body, exactly-maxBytes boundary, maxBytes+1 overflow → null, empty body, stream failing mid-read → null, cap pinned at 1 MiB). **Kotlin proof is CI-only**: this VM has no Java/Gradle/Android SDK/kotlinc (same as Fix 7 and Fix 16), so the new unit tests and the Android build are verified by the PR's CI checks, not locally. There is no local fail-without-fix run for the Kotlin tests for the same reason; the pure functions are pinned and the service wiring (refresh merge, fetchList failure path, request TransportFailure mapping) was verified by reading against the existing callers field by field.
- NREP-3 has no JVM test by construction (the observable behaviour is a `SharedPreferences` write; no Robolectric, no fake `Context` harness in this repo) — verification is the one-line guard deletion plus the caller analysis above, CI build, and CI unit tests staying green.

Deviations: the Xingchi gateway's `/v1/responses` endpoint was still down (sustained 503 since ~18:10 CET 2026-11-21, ~27 hours by this round), so Sol never started; the user explicitly instructed at ~20:15 CET 2026-11-22 to continue without Sol. The parent implemented the fix directly (same pattern as 11b/11c/12b/12c/14b/15/15b/15c/16) from the prep doc (`~/workspace/wildfire-fix-prep/fix17-prep.md`, 5 open decisions — all resolved to the doc's recommendations: fetchList-side heuristic, persist-empty, 1 MiB cap with TransportFailure mapping, error bodies keep HttpError, pure-function extraction) and the existing code. No scope expansion. Remaining for **PR #148**: independent review, push, PR creation, CI watch, merge.

### Fix 17b — explicit type arguments in the empty-list test (CI compile error)

The first CI watch on 92684a7 returned DO NOT MERGE: `build-and-test` failed in `:app:compileDebugUnitTestKotlin` (no tests ran) with six errors, all in `RefreshPolicyTest.kt:21` — "Cannot infer type for this parameter / Not enough information to infer type argument for 'T'" at columns 69, 82, 95. Cause: the three bare `emptyList()` calls cannot infer `T` against the `List<*>?` parameter (a star projection is not a concrete type for inference); only the first call had an explicit `<Int>`. Fix: explicit `<Int>` on all four calls. The parent and the independent reviewer had both read the test as compilable — the compiler proved otherwise, and the mistake is owned plainly here. (The watch also noted CodeRabbit Minor 4660024560 — a body with one valid + one invalid row returns the reduced list as a success, dropping the unparseable row from the snapshot. Disposition, parent's decision: documented follow-up, NOT fixed in this part. Per-item skipping is deliberate defensive parsing — one bad row must not poison the whole feed; whole-response rejection would stall that endpoint forever if a row is persistently malformed; the dropped row has no valid coordinates and can never be displayed, mapped, or proximity-checked, so the snapshot loses nothing usable; and surfacing skip counts needs the `Models.kt` contract change the program deliberately deferred. A future `Models.kt` review could surface skip counts.)

## Fix 18 — make native emergency queues durable before confirming submission (NREP-4 + NREP-5, both Major)

Fixes NREP-4 and NREP-5 (report lines 1355–1356, both in `AppRepository.kt`). NREP-4: `persistQueues` took a snapshot and then passed it to an unsynchronized writer, so two writers for the same kind could install their files in the opposite order to the order their snapshots were taken — the older snapshot won the disk, silently losing the newer entries from disk while they remained in memory (visible only after process death, when the restore reads the stale file). All writers also shared one tmp filename per kind (`queue_<kind>.json.tmp`), so concurrent writers could interleave bytes into the same tmp file and rename a torn file into place. NREP-5: `persistQueueFile` stopped serializing when cumulative payload length exceeded 12,000,000 chars, so legal ~700,000-char SOS audio payloads exhausted the budget after ~17 entries — below the queue capacity of 60 — and the newer accepted entries stayed memory-only (only `Log.w` reported it) while `sendSos` still called `onDone(true, …, OFFLINE_QUEUED_MSG)` promising storage and automatic sending. Process death lost those omitted requests. Disk write failures were similarly swallowed without changing the callback outcome.

### The fix

NREP-4 — serialize snapshot + write as one atomic step (`AppRepository.kt`): one lock per queue kind (`reportQueuePersistLock`, `sosQueuePersistLock`); `persistQueues` now takes the snapshot AND writes the file inside the same per-kind lock (`persistQueueFileLocked`), so two writers for one kind are totally ordered — the later snapshot is always the later install. The lock also fixes the shared-tmp interference (same-kind writers can no longer interleave in the tmp file); unique tmp names were considered and rejected — they leave the stale-overwrite race intact, and are unnecessary once the lock is held. The `renameTo` fallback is fixed in the same function: if the rename fails, it is retried once and, if it still fails, the old file stays intact and the persist reports failure — the old direct `f.writeText(...)` fallback (a crash mid-write would corrupt the queue) is deleted. Lock-ordering discipline is documented in the code: the persist lock is the OUTER lock, the queue monitor is taken only inside it via `snapshot()`; no code path calls `persistQueues()` while holding a queue monitor (verified: `enqueue`/`commit`/`reserveDue` never call persist; the drain loop commits — releasing the monitor — before persisting).

NREP-5 — honest budget + conditional confirmation (`AppRepository.kt`, `OfflineQueue.kt`): (1) `MAX_PERSIST_TOTAL_CHARS` raised 12,000,000 → 48,000,000 (60 entries × ~700,000 chars ≈ 42 M, rounded up with headroom for JSON escaping; the derivation is in the code comment). Worst case two files × ~48 MB (+ one tmp each during write) is the honest price of the "durable queue" promise. `MAX_PERSIST_ENTRY_CHARS` (800,000) is unchanged — a legal max SOS audio (~700 k) fits; it only rejects runaway garbage. A report with a near-max photo (500 k-char data URI — the builder cap in ApiPayloads) still fits under it. (2) `persistQueueFile` now returns a `QueuePersistResult` (persisted / omitted / failed; `durable` is true only when every entry in the snapshot is on disk) instead of swallowing failures in `Log.w`. The budget/omission selection is extracted into the pure, JVM-tested `OfflineQueue.selectPersistable` (oldest first; oversized entries skipped; once the budget is exceeded, that entry and every newer one is omitted; an entry exactly at the boundary persists). The two offline branches persist FIRST and confirm only on durable: `submitReport` calls `onDone(true, OFFLINE_QUEUED_MSG)` only when the reports persist is durable, else `onDone(false, QUEUE_PERSIST_FAILED_MSG)` ("تعذر حفظ الطلب على الجهاز — سيُحاول إرساله طالما التطبيق مفتوح"); `sendSos` likewise, and does NOT set `queuedWhileOffline = true` on the failure path (that flag is cleared by the drain loop only when the queue empties, and it would keep promising a send that process death will cancel). In both cases the entry stays in memory — the drain loop can still send it while the app lives (best effort). The drain loop now logs an incomplete persist (omitted/failed counts per kind) and keeps running — a persist failure must never kill the 20 s loop, and `persistQueues()` never throws (the `runCatching` no-throw property is preserved). `persistSnapshot()` failures stay out of scope (Fix 17 owns that function); the dispatcher is left alone (`Dispatchers.Default`, per the prep recommendation); `OfflineQueue`, `readQueueFile`/`restoreQueues` (no format change, no migration), and the mesh broadcast calls are untouched.

### Verification

- `npm run lint`: 0 diagnostics.
- `npm run build`: pass.
- Full `npm run test:server`: **875/875 passed**.
- Full `npm run test:react`: **1042/1042 passed** (the dataset-outages:114 timeout flake did not hit this run).
- `git diff --check`: passed.
- New tests in `OfflineQueueTest` (6 cases pinning `selectPersistable`: a full queue of 60 × 700 k-char payloads fits the new 48 M budget with 0 omitted; the old 12 M budget held 17 and dropped 43 — the fail-without-fix shape; one 900 k-char entry among small ones is omitted and the rest persist; over the total budget only the newest tail is omitted; empty list; an entry exactly at the boundary persists and one char over does not). **Kotlin proof is CI-only**: this VM has no Java/Gradle/Android SDK/kotlinc (same as Fix 7, Fix 16 and Fix 17), so the new unit tests and the Android build are verified by the PR's CI checks, not locally. There is no local fail-without-fix run for the Kotlin tests for the same reason; the pure function is pinned and the lock + callback-gating wiring was verified by reading against the three call sites and the two fragment callbacks field by field (both fragments already handle `onDone(false, …)` with an error toast via the existing `report_failed_fmt` / `sos_failed_fmt` branches, and the `ok && userError == OFFLINE_QUEUED_MSG` branches only fire on success).
- The lock and the callback gating have no JVM test by construction (no `AppRepositoryTest` harness; `AppRepository` needs an Android `Context`) — verified by code review + CI build, stated honestly.

Deviations: the Xingchi gateway's `/v1/responses` endpoint was still down (sustained 503 since ~18:10 CET 2026-11-21, ~28 hours by this round), so Sol never started; the user explicitly instructed at ~20:15 CET 2026-11-22 to continue without Sol. The parent implemented the fix directly (same pattern as 11b/11c/12b/12c/14b/15/15b/15c/16/17) from the prep doc (`~/workspace/wildfire-fix-prep/fix18-prep.md`, 4 open decisions — all resolved to the doc's recommendations: keep-in-memory + honest `onDone(false)` (D1), accept the 48 M budget (D2), leave the dispatcher alone (D3), `persistSnapshot()` failures stay out of scope (D4)) and the existing code. No scope expansion. ### Fix 18b — one pending SOS per device: reuse the queued key on a re-press (CodeRabbit Major 4660191944, from #149's watch; revised per Major 4660269795)

The first CI watch on 7c4eb34 returned DO NOT MERGE (all own checks + both Analyzes green, CodeRabbit clean except 2 Majors, both verified VALID). Major 1: the new failure path tells the user the save failed while the entry stays in the in-memory queue and the drain keeps retrying it — and the SOS button re-enables immediately (`SosFragment.kt:119`, `sosButton?.isEnabled = !snap.sos.sending`), so the user naturally presses SOS again. The new submission gets a new `clientGeneratedId`, so the server cannot match it to the original; once the newer SOS is admitted, the queued original's retries hit the server's duplicate guard (`createSosWithAdmission` returns `"duplicate"` when `acceptedAt - lastAcceptedAt < DUPLICATE_WINDOW_MS`, 5 min, `server/routes/sos.ts:155`, `server/fs.ts:285`), the idempotency replay finds nothing for the original key (it was never stored), and the route returns 409 "An SOS from this device was already received recently" (`sos.ts:510`). The drain treated 409 as failure, not delivery (only `is2xx` counts), so the entry survived with exponential backoff (30 s → 60 s → 120 s → 240 s); the 5th attempt, after the 240 s backoff (~450 s cumulative), falls outside the 5-minute window → admission returns `"created"` → a second stored SOS for the same person. The parent verified every link of this chain against the code and the server before fixing it. FIRST ATTEMPT (withdrawn): in the drain loop, a 409 whose extracted error message matched a pure `isAlreadyReceivedRecentlyMessage` helper was treated as delivered — the entry dropped via the normal commit path. The re-watch on 0bab8ab returned DO NOT MERGE again: CodeRabbit Major 4660269795 (VALID) — the drop was over-broad, because the server's window is PER DEVICE, so a DISTINCT queued emergency inside the window got the same 409 and was dropped without ever being stored. REVISED FIX (source, not drain): `sendSos` reuses the queued entry's key on a re-press — one pending SOS per device. `sosQueue.snapshot().firstOrNull()?.key` is reused when present, a fresh key minted only when nothing is queued; the failure branch refreshes the WAITING entry's payload under the same key via the new `OfflineQueue.replacePayload` (attempts/backoff survive; in-flight entries are owned by the drain and never touched). The drain's 409-drop and the matcher are DELETED; the drain keeps the old retry-forever behaviour — a 409 retries with backoff and is admitted once the window passes, so nothing queued is ever dropped on a 409. Known limitation: a cold start can still restore several pre-fix entries; the first (oldest) key is reused and the rest keep the old retry behaviour — no loss, just no new key. The drain wiring has no JVM test by construction (no `AppRepositoryTest` harness for the loop) — verified by reading, stated honestly.

### Fix 18c — sync the queue file and its directory before reporting durability (CodeRabbit Major 4660191946, from #149's watch; revised per Major 4660269789)

Major 2: the persist path wrote the temp file with `tmp.writeText(...)` and no `sync()`/`force()` anywhere, so a successful rename permitted `durable == true` before the update reached permanent storage — rename is atomic replacement, not durability, and battery loss before writeback loses a queue the callback reported as saved. This directly targets this part's own promise ("durable BEFORE confirming submission"). FIRST ATTEMPT (withdrawn): the write was extracted into the pure companion helper `writeFileDurably(tmp, target, bytes)` — write via `FileOutputStream` + `fd.sync()`, rename (retry once), then sync the containing directory via `FileInputStream(dir).fd.sync()`. The re-watch on 0bab8ab returned DO NOT MERGE again: build-and-test FAILED (337 tests, 2 failed — both `AppRepositoryTest` success cases, `writeFileDurably` returning false; the failure test and the matcher tests passed) AND CodeRabbit Major 4660269789 (VALID) — `FileInputStream(dir)` is rejected on Android (IoBridge) and on the JVM (OpenJDK's directory check), so the directory sync threw on every normal write. The parent confirmed the diagnosis from the signed-in job log (run 45075534346, job 137371802668, step 7: not a compile error, `compileDebugUnitTestKotlin` green; the two failures are exactly the success-path asserts at AppRepositoryTest.kt:64 and :78). REVISED FIX: the directory sync is `syncDirectory` — `android.system.Os.open` + `Os.fsync` + `Os.close` (minSdk 26, Os is API 21+) — with a seam: `writeFileDurably(..., syncDir = { dir -> syncDirectory(dir) })`; production passes the real one, JVM tests pass a fake (Os is a stub in unit tests, so the real sync is proven by compilation + device behaviour, not by a unit test). Failure contract corrected (CodeRabbit Minor 4660269800 — the first 18c text got this wrong): a failure BEFORE the rename leaves the old target intact; a failure AFTER the rename (directory sync) leaves the NEW contents in place with unknown durability; either way the return is false and the caller must not report the queue as durable — the persist log now says "durability not confirmed" instead of "keeping previous file". New JVM tests in `AppRepositoryTest` (4 cases: the durable write lands exact bytes with no temp left, a second write replaces the first, a failed write keeps the previous file intact, and a failed directory sync reports failure while the new file stays in place) run the helper against real temp files on Linux CI with a fake dir sync. Kotlin proof is CI-only, as for all Kotlin in this program.

### Fix 18f — never report offline queuing for a submission that was not stored (CodeRabbit Major 4660332342, from #149's re-watch on b15ab7b)

The third CI watch on b15ab7b returned DO NOT MERGE again — build-and-test GREEN at last (the Os seam fixed the two AppRepositoryTest failures, confirming the diagnosis), all previous Majors resolved in code, but 1 NEW Major, verified VALID by the coordinator: when a re-pressed SOS finds its key in flight, both admission calls return false (`replacePayload` only searches waiting entries; `enqueue` refuses in-flight keys) — yet the code then called `persistQueues()` and reported `onDone(true, OFFLINE_QUEUED_MSG)` whenever `durable` was true. `durable = !failed && omitted == 0` is VACUOUS for an empty snapshot (an empty queue persists as an empty array), so in the poisoned interleave — entry at 7 failed attempts, in flight, re-press lands, drain's commit drops it after 8 attempts — neither POST succeeded and no payload is stored anywhere, yet the user is told "saved, will send automatically." Fix: `QueuePersistResult` gains `persistedKeys` (the keys actually written to the file, empty on write failure), and the queued-success path requires all three: the submission was admitted (`replacePayload || enqueue`), its key is in `persistedKeys`, and its key is still in the queue snapshot — in BOTH `sendSos` and `submitReport` (the report path had the same vacuous-durable hole: the drain could reserve a fresh entry between the enqueue and the persist; a fresh entry cannot be poison-dropped on its first round, so the key check alone suffices there). Known residual, stated honestly: an admitted entry at 7 failed attempts can still be poison-dropped by the drain after a truthful report — the pre-existing MAX_ATTEMPTS doctrine ("poisoned — dropped, never hoarded"), out of scope for this part; changing it is a separate product decision, not a queue bug. The drain wiring has no JVM test by construction — verified by reading, stated honestly.

### Fix 19 — handle native camera lifecycle and image limits (NCAM-1 + NCAM-2 + NPHOTO-1, all Major)

NPHOTO-1 (`PhotoPipeline.kt`): the budget was enforced on the wrong quantity — `MAX_BYTES` 450,000 decoded JPEG bytes becomes ~600,023 data-URI chars, over `ApiPayloads`' 500,000-char gate, so a photo accepted as attached made the report unsubmittable. Fix: `MAX_BYTES` → **374,982** (the largest byte count whose data URI fits: 499,976 + 23 = 499,999; 375,000 does NOT fit at 500,023) + the KDoc corrected to state the derivation. `compressWithinBudget`'s loop is untouched (D2: constant fix, not the structural reframe; D3: local constant, not derived from `ApiPayloads`). New JVM tests in `PhotoPipelineTest` pin the constant against the wire gate and the exact boundary (374,982 fits, 374,983 and 375,000 do not); reverting the constant to 450,000 fails the gate test (600,023). Interaction noted (D1): with the smaller budget more bitmaps need quality 20 to fit, so the NPHOTO-2 gap (the floor quality is never attempted — the loop exits once quality becomes 20, despite its KDoc's "always attempted" claim) becomes slightly more visible; the loop is left alone for NPHOTO-2's own part.

NCAM-1 (`CameraCaptureFragment.kt`): the CameraX callbacks could fire after the user closed the camera — `onImageSaved` touched `viewLifecycleOwner` (throws after `onDestroyView`) and `onError` called `requireContext()` (throws once detached), while the existing `isAdded` guard sat inside the background completion block, too late. Fix: both callbacks gate on `isAdded` first (dropping the result / skipping the toast, resetting `captureInFlight` on the save path), and `processAndDeliver` uses the fragment `lifecycleScope` (already imported) instead of the view scope — it lives until `onDestroy`, so the delivery launch itself can never throw for a destroyed view; the `isAdded` check inside the launch is unchanged. No JVM seam exists (no fragment test harness in this repo; no Robolectric) — verified by review + CI build, stated honestly; no instrumented test (D5). Rotation during stamping now survives instead of dropping the capture (a strict improvement; `setFragmentResult` + `popBack()` stay `isAdded`-guarded).

NCAM-2 (`CameraCaptureFragment.kt` + `PhotoPipeline.kt`): `processAndDeliver` overwrote the original JPEG in place (`file.outputStream()` truncates on open) and discarded `compress`'s boolean, so the "deliver the raw capture" fallback delivered a truncated file. Fix: the new pure `PhotoPipeline.writeAtomically(target, write)` helper writes to a same-directory `<name>.stamped` temp and renames only when the writer reports success AND the temp is non-empty; any failure deletes the temp and returns false, so the original is never opened for writing and is delivered raw, genuinely intact. New JVM tests in `PhotoPipelineTest` with real temp files (success replaces + no temp left; writer-false and writer-throws leave the original byte-identical + temp deleted). Fail-without-fix is by construction: the false/throw cases assert the original survives, which the old in-place write violates by definition. No stale-temp cleanup (D4 — cacheDir leftovers are benign). `ReportFragment.kt`, `ApiPayloads.kt`, and the server are untouched.

Remaining for Fix 19: push, PR creation, CI watch, merge.

Independent review (fix19-review.md): verdict **CLEAN** — 0 Critical/Major/Minor; all three findings verified closed with file:line evidence (incl. the NPHOTO-1 arithmetic re-computed independently: 374,982 → 499,999 is the largest fitting byte count; 400,000 → 533,359 matches the dissection report). 2 doc-only Nits, both addressed in c1494d1: the onImageSaved comment no longer cites viewLifecycleOwner; the PhotoPipeline KDoc no longer says only the sampling math is unit-tested. Kotlin compile + the 5 new JVM tests are CI-only proof (no local JVM).

### Fix 19b — attempt the floor quality before returning null (CodeRabbit Major from #150's first watch, VALID)

The first CI watch on 863f9b9 returned DO NOT MERGE: all own checks + both Analyzes green, the 5 new Kotlin tests pass in CI, instrumented = the known boot-timeout flake (non-blocking) — but CodeRabbit found a valid Major. The loop compressed at 80/65/50/35 and then exited when quality became 20 (`quality > FLOOR_QUALITY`), so quality 20 was never attempted even though the KDoc claimed "the floor quality itself is always attempted" — that is dissection finding NPHOTO-2, which Fix 19 deliberately deferred. The deferral was wrong to keep: with the new 374,982 budget an image encoding to 400,000 bytes at quality 35 and 350,000 at quality 20 fits the budget yet returned `null`, and `ReportFragment.ingestImage` then showed `report_photo_too_big` and dropped the photo (with the old 450,000 budget the same image returned the 400,000-byte result at quality 35 — Fix 19 created this failure mode). Fix: one character, `>` → `>=`. Bounded: after compressing at 20, quality becomes 5 and the loop ends — it never compresses below the floor, and the KDoc claim is now true (its "q > 20" wording corrected to "q >= 20"). No JVM test can pin the loop directly (`Bitmap` is stubbed on the JVM test classpath) — verified by code reading + CI build, stated honestly. NPHOTO-2 is now closed by this fix, not by a separate part.

### Follow-up (documented, not a blocker — CodeRabbit Minor from #150's first watch, VALID)

Failed stamped write reports a negative pre-scan: on write failure the `else null` branch discards the scan that already ran on the stamped pixels, so the bundle sends `RESULT_PRESCAN_PRESENT=false` / `RESULT_PRESCAN_CONFIDENCE=0` and `ReportFragment.showCaptureTelemetry` renders `prescan_negative_fmt` ("no fire-like shades observed (0%)") for a raw file that was never scanned. Closing it needs a new bundle key + string + rendering in `showCaptureTelemetry` — outside Fix 19's scope (per the program's precedent for Minors: Fix 16 duplicate-display, Fix 17 per-item-skip). Harm is bounded: both pre-scan strings carry the "estimative only" disclaimer, and the same null→negative mapping pre-exists for decode failures.

### Fix 20 — require fresh native coordinates at capture, route, report and SOS time (NCAM-3 + NMAP-3 + NREPORT-1 + NSOS-1, all Major)

Shared root cause (all four): `LocationEngine.currentFix()` returns the retained `lastFix` with no freshness test, and none of these four fragments called the freshness gate that already exists. The fix is four small call-site gates on one shared contract — no new location machinery, no `LocationEngine` change.

New shared seam: `LocationLogic.isUsableFix(fix, nowMs)` — true when a fix is a real recent measurement (≤ 10 min, same skew rule as `isFreshFix`). New JVM tests in `LocationLogicTest` pin it (null → false; 30 s → true; 61 s → usable but NOT fresh; exactly 10 min → true; 10 min + 1 ms → false; far-future → false for both bars; small future drift inside the skew → true for both). The 60 s `isFreshFix` boundaries were already pinned by existing tests, so no new tests for the camera/map gates.

NCAM-3 (`CameraCaptureFragment.kt`): `takePhoto` stamps only a fresh fix (`freshFix = fix.takeIf { isFreshFix(it, nowMs) }` — passing null is the existing no-fix path in `buildStamp`); `updateHud` prints a stale fix as `gps_stale_fmt` ("آخر موقع معروف") instead of coordinates; `updateAlignmentLine` skips the `crossCheck` unless the fix is fresh and a stale fix falls through to the existing `camera_alignment_no_fix` branch. `processAndDeliver` untouched (Fix 19's territory).

NMAP-3 (`MapFragment.kt`): the fix time now travels with the position (`lastUserFixMs`, set in `onFix`) + a `hasFreshOrigin()` helper (single gate, same bounds as `isFreshFix`). `onFix` keeps the marker visible at the last-known position but only rides the view on a fresh fix (follow / first-jump / outside-view animations gated); the status line switches to `gps_stale_fmt` when stale. `fetchEvacuationRoute` refuses a stale origin (same `route_need_fix` toast as the no-fix case) before any OSRM call or straight-line fallback. Follow and recenter buttons refuse to jump on a stale fix (`gps_searching` toast). `updateUserMarkerRotation` untouched (NMAP-5, out of scope); `renderThreats`, the AI briefing cache (NMAP-2), the CARTO toggle (NMAP-4) untouched.

NREPORT-1 (`ReportFragment.kt`): `submit` requires `isUsableFix` after the null check — refusal happens BEFORE disabling the button or calling `submitReport`, so nothing is queued either. `onLocation` labels a too-old-to-submit fix with `gps_stale_fmt` so the user sees WHY submit refuses. Autofill deliberately NOT re-gated (v2.19.0 lesson); `ApiPayloads` untouched (D4: no fix timestamp in the payload — protocol change, recorded as a follow-up).

NSOS-1 (`SosFragment.kt`): respects the documented header policy (send even if stale) and fixes the disclosure instead. `renderLocation` reordered so a nonnull fix is shown with its age (`sos_location_fmt`) — PROVIDERS_OFF no longer hides it. (NO_PERMISSION still precedes the fix branch, as before: with no permission there is no live fix to show.) `doSend` marks a stale fix (beyond the 10-min usable window) with the new `sos_stale_fix_fmt` string ("⚠ موقع قديم — منذ %1$d ث — %2$s"), parallel to the existing no-fix marker. `startCountdown`/`cancelCountdown` untouched (NSOS-2, Fix 23); recording path and profile persistence untouched.

Decisions (all = the prep doc's recommendations): D1 60 s `isFreshFix` for camera stamp + map display + route origin, 10-min `isUsableFix` for report submit + SOS, always with age disclosure; D2 keep sending the SOS with the stale marker (never silently drop an SOS); D3 wording reuses existing strings (`gps_stale_fmt`, `route_need_fix`, `report_need_fix`, `gps_searching`) + one new marker string; D4 fix timestamp in the payload out of scope (follow-up); D5 keep the last-known marker visible labelled stale, refuse follow/recenter/route on a stale fix.

Fragment wiring (which branch calls which gate) has no JVM seam — proven by the CI Android build + code review, stated honestly (same pattern as Fix 16/17). The CI gate for this part: `gradle test assembleDebug` (all unit tests incl. the new `LocationLogicTest` cases) + `assembleRelease` + Analyze (java-kotlin) + the usual TS checks.

Independent review (2026-11-23 ~02:01 CET, `~/workspace/wildfire-fix-prep/fix20-review.md`): verdict CLEAN — 0 Critical/Major/Minor, 3 Nits, all addressed: (1) FIX_PLAN wording corrected — NO_PERMISSION still precedes the fix branch in `renderLocation`; (2) `doSend`'s `ageS` clamped at 0 (a far-future fix would otherwise print a negative age); the same unclamped pattern pre-exists in `renderLocation` and is left as-is (out of scope); (3) the report location line now shows the age for usable fixes too (`report_location_line_fmt` gains "— منذ %4$d ث", clamped at 0) — D1's "always with age disclosure" now holds in full. The reviewer also verified all other `lastUserLatLng` consumers: `drawRings`/`drawCone` are display-only (consistent with D5) and the AI-briefing send is NMAP-2 territory (Fix 22).

### Fix 20b — refresh the HUD when location freshness changes (CodeRabbit Minor 4661008525 on PR #151, VALID)

The first watch on d4bf3e0 returned DO NOT MERGE: all own checks + both Analyzes green, but CodeRabbit found that `locationListener` called only `updateAlignmentLine()` — `updateHud()` (where the NCAM-3 freshness branch lives) otherwise runs only from the compass path, the stamp toggle, the sliders, and once at view creation. On a device without a rotation-vector sensor, `startCompass()` returns early, so a fresh fix arriving after creation never appeared on the status line and fresh coordinates stayed on screen past the 60 s boundary. Display-only (the stamp re-checks freshness at capture; the alignment line was already re-evaluated) — Minor, not Major, but in Fix 20's own scope (the 16d precedent), not a documented follow-up.

Fix (one line): the listener now calls `updateHud()` instead of `updateAlignmentLine()` — `updateHud()` already ends with `updateAlignmentLine()`, so alignment behavior is preserved exactly. The second half of CodeRabbit's suggestion (a scheduled refresh at the freshness-expiry boundary) is unnecessary: `LocationEngine` re-notifies listeners every 3 s while FIXED (`statusPoller`, `publish()` notifies on every tick while state is FIXED), so the 3 s ticks re-evaluate the `isFreshFix` branch continuously while the view is active.

Follow-up (not a blocker, recorded from the second watch on eec8918): CodeRabbit Minor (review 6015705510) — a stale fix makes `takePhoto` pass `alignment = null`, and `alignmentStampLines(null)` returns `"NO EXISTING REPORT WITHIN BEARING/RANGE"` although no search ran. The same null→negative mapping pre-exists for the no-fix / no-heading cases, so this is not a regression of the NCAM-3 guarantee (no stale coordinates are stamped — only the alignment verdict line is misleading). Closing it properly needs a new "check unavailable" result threaded through the renderer plus a new stamp string — CodeRabbit itself labels it "Heavy lift" — the same shape as the Fix 19 pre-scan Minor, recorded here as a follow-up rather than blocking the merge.

### Fix 20 — merged as PR #151 (merge 3346e3e, 2026-11-23 ~03:22 CET, parents 59d1eda + e924836, 8 files +183/−22, 11 commits)

### Fix 21 — correct native heading, dashboard weather and location freshness (NHEAD-1 + NOBS-1 + NOBS-2, all Major)

NHEAD-1 (`HeadingEngine.kt`/`HeadingLogic.kt`): every live sensor heading was suppressed — `start()` forced a publish while `smoothed` was null (storing `lastNotified = null`), and every later sample computed `prev = lastNotified ?: value`, i.e. prev IS the current value, so the delta was 0 < 1° and `publish` returned without notifying anyone. Fix: new pure gate `HeadingLogic.shouldPublishHeading(prevNotified, current, minDeltaDeg)` — null current never publishes, null previous ALWAYS publishes, otherwise the 1° gate applies; `publish` is a 3-line call-site change. `start()` and `addListener` (late-join replay) unchanged; smoothing/throttle untouched; both consumer fragments benefit automatically. New JVM tests in `HeadingLogicTest` pin the gate (`(null, 42.0)` → true is the fail-without-fix proof; boundary 1.0° publishes; wrap cases).

NOBS-1 (`ObservatoryFragment.kt`): GPS-loss status did not invalidate the dashboard's position-based danger info — `updateRadar` treated any retained `currentFix()` as live (`userHasFix = true`, blips/wilaya/nearest-threat from the old coordinates) and the proximity banner did the same, while only the GPS chip applied `isFreshFix`. Fix: `updateRadar()` (the single choke point, covering both call paths) gates on `liveFix = fix.takeIf { isFreshFix(it, now) }` — stale → empty radar + `radar_awareness_waiting` ("بانتظار تحديد موقعك…"); the banner block gates the same way (`fix = currentFix().takeIf { isFreshFix(it, now) }`, stale → banner stays GONE). 60 s bar (decision D1 — the finding cites `isFreshFix` and the chip already uses it). `renderGps`'s chip logic untouched; `LocationEngine` untouched. Note: the empty-radar path now shows `radar_awareness_waiting` for BOTH the no-fix and the stale-fix case (they share one branch) — the wording is accurate for both.

NOBS-2 (`AppRepository.kt` + `RadarV2.kt` + `ObservatoryFragment.kt`): weather was fetched once per view (`weatherRequested` set before the request, never cleared) and never aged (`WeatherNow` has no timestamp), so one success drove guidance indefinitely and one failure blocked retries forever. Fix: `Snapshot` gains `weatherFetchedAtMs` (stamped on success in `fetchWeatherAt`; carried across `refreshAll`'s copy — the single most likely regression in this part, verified by reading); new pure `RadarV2.weatherFresh(fetchedAtMs, nowMs)` + `WEATHER_MAX_AGE_MS = 10 min` (same shape as `aiCacheFresh`); the fragment replaces the flag with a `lastWeatherAttemptMs` throttle (set before the call, MapFragment's pattern) — fetch only when the fix is fresh AND the weather is stale AND 10 min passed since the last attempt; the weather line hides once the reading ages out; `updateRadar` passes a stale wind as `null` (existing `RadarView.setData` path — the drift cone simply does not draw). `WeatherNow`/`Models.kt`/`parseWeather` untouched; `fetchWeatherAt`'s signature/`onDone` contract unchanged; `persistSnapshot()` gains no weather (Fix 17's contract). New JVM tests in `RadarV2Test` pin `weatherFresh` (9 min fresh, exactly 10 min stale, 0 false, future-stamped false).

Decisions (all = the prep doc's recommendations): D1 60 s `isFreshFix` for NOBS-1; D2 timestamp in the repository `Snapshot`; D3 stale-radar wording reuses `radar_awareness_waiting`; D4 time-only refresh (10 min) — the distance-based re-fetch (needs fetched coordinates stored) is a recorded follow-up; D5 MapFragment's wind chip + cone (`updateWindChip`, `drawCone` read `snap.weather` directly and would still show stale wind) is a follow-up part, not Fix 21; D6 `WEATHER_MAX_AGE_MS` lives in `RadarV2` next to `AI_CACHE_TTL_MS`.

Fragment wiring (which branch calls which gate) has no JVM seam — proven only by the CI Android build + code review; no emulator/device run was performed in this VM. The CI gate for this part: `gradle test assembleDebug` (all unit tests incl. the new `HeadingLogicTest` + `RadarV2Test` cases) + `assembleRelease` + Analyze (java-kotlin) + the usual TS checks.

**Fix 21b** (independent review Minor 1, blocking): mounting with already-fresh snapshot weather left the weather line hidden and unpopulated — the trigger skips the fetch when the reading is fresh, the layout starts the line `gone`, and the text was written only in the fetch callback. Fix: `renderGps` now renders the fresh snapshot weather into the line itself (text + VISIBLE when `weatherFresh`, else GONE), replacing the hide-only display gate.

Independent review verdict NOT CLEAN (0 Critical, 0 Major, 1 Minor, 2 Nits — `~/workspace/wildfire-fix-prep/fix21-review.md`): NHEAD-1 closed, NOBS-1 closed (all 5 position uses on `liveFix`, counted), NOBS-2 closed except the Minor above (R7 carry verified NOT present, `weatherRequested` 0 references); the Minor fixed by 21b and both doc Nits addressed in FIX_PLAN. Local gates re-run after 21b: lint 0, build pass, server 875/875, React 1042/1042.

**Fix 21c** (first CI watch on PR #152, DO NOT MERGE — review 6016045404, all 3 comments verified in code by the parent): **Major 4661373429** — the banner's freshness was re-evaluated only in `render()`, which runs only on repository state emissions; offline `startPolling` skips `refreshAll`, so no emission ever fires and a stale proximity warning stayed on screen after GPS loss (location events drive `renderGps`, never `render`). **Minor 4661373418** — `refreshAll` captured `prev` then did four blocking endpoint calls before copying `weather`/`weatherFetchedAtMs` from `prev`, so a `fetchWeatherAt` landing in between was reverted. **Minor 4661373422** — the weather line was touched only in `renderGps`, and the engine suppresses unchanged non-FIXED states, so after the one status-change notification the line stayed visible past `WEATHER_MAX_AGE_MS`. Fix: the banner block is extracted into `updateProximityBanner(snap)` and the weather line into `updateWeatherLine(snap, now)`; one `refreshFreshnessDisplays()` re-runs weather line + banner + radar, driven by `renderGps`, by `render` (banner + radar) and by a new 10 s `Handler` tick started in `onResume`, stopped in `onPause` and removed in `onDestroyView` (the `Handler(Looper.getMainLooper())` pattern already used by `LocationEngine`, `NativeMainActivity` and `SosFragment`); `refreshAll`'s copy drops both weather assignments — the copy keeps the receiver's current values, so a mid-refresh fetch survives, and with no overlap current == prev so the R7 carry still holds. All own checks were green on the first watch (build-and-test incl. the new Kotlin tests, both Analyzes); the instrumented check was still in_progress at the last poll and cannot change the verdict. ### Fix 22 — correct native route clearance and scope guidance to location (NMAP-1 + NMAP-2, both Major)

NMAP-1 (`MapFragment.kt` + `GeoMath.kt`): the evacuation clearance measured the wrong geometry — `fetchEvacuationRoute` passed each road vertex as the point and the fire locations as the "polyline" to `minDistanceToPolylineM`, which connects adjacent list entries into segments; a fire on the road between two vertices was missed, two fires straddling a vertex invented a zero-clearance segment. The wrong value drove `rankRoutes`, the cross/safe verdict, chip colours and auto-selection. Fix: new pure helper `GeoMath.minDistanceBetweenPolylineAndPointsM(route, points)` — min over fires of each fire's distance to the road polyline (empty route → NaN, fails open to "crosses" per `RouteOption`; no fires → MAX_VALUE); the call site is a single call, the threat picture / `renderRouteChips` / `selectRoute(0)` unchanged. New JVM tests in `GeoMathTest` pin the report's two scenarios (fire on the road → < 1 m; straddling fires → > 10 km) plus edge cases; the fail-without-fix shape is worked out in the test comments (old shape ≈ 8,926 m "safe" for A, 0.0 false "crosses" for B — both assertions fail against it). `minDistanceToPolylineM` itself untouched, its tests stay green.

NMAP-2 (`MapFragment.kt` + `ObservatoryApp.kt` + `RadarV2.kt`): the AI safety guidance cache checked only text presence + age before reading coordinates, and the app cache held text/time only — a briefing fetched at A was served at B for up to an hour, and a no-location briefing kept being served once GPS appeared. Fix: `ObservatoryApp` stores `aiBriefingKey` next to the text/time; new pure `RadarV2.aiBriefingCacheHit(cachedKey, currentKey, cachedAtMs, nowMs)` (key equality + TTL); `onAiBriefingRequested` reads `lastUserLatLng` BEFORE the lookup, builds the current key via the already-tested `aiCacheKey` (exact coordinates, `lang "ar"`, hour bucket `now / AI_CACHE_TTL_MS`), hits only on key match, stores the key together with the text on success, and scopes the failure fallback to the same key (a different-location stale briefing must not show when the fresh request failed — `ai_failed` instead). The 5 s spacing is untouched; the request itself still sends `lastUserLatLng` as today (D3 — a briefing fetched for stale coordinates is still correctly keyed under those coordinates). New JVM tests in `RadarV2Test` pin `aiBriefingCacheHit` (same key + fresh → hit; moved coords → miss; stale → miss; null cached key → miss; hour boundary → miss; no-location vs located → miss).

Decisions (all = the prep doc's recommendations): D1 exact coordinates via `aiCacheKey` as-is (web parity; the 5 s spacing bounds the refetch rate for a moving user); D2 key-scoped failure fallback; D3 keep today's request behaviour; D4 safe-let in the hit branch (the key/text pairing is a convention, not a type — `!!` rejected); D5 helper shape (only shape with a failing-without-fix JVM test); D6 helper lives in `GeoMath`.

Fragment wiring (the `MapFragment` call site + the `onAiBriefingRequested` restructure) has no JVM seam — proven by the CI Android build + code review; no emulator/device run was performed in this VM. The CI gate for this part: `gradle test assembleDebug` (all unit tests incl. the new `GeoMathTest` + `RadarV2Test` cases) + `assembleRelease` + Analyze (java-kotlin) + the usual TS checks.

**Fix 22b** (independent review Minor 1, blocking — a spec flaw in the prep doc's §3.3 sketch, not a deviation): the failure fallback compared the *current* `app.aiBriefingKey` against `currentKey` but showed the *tap-time* `cached` text. With two overlapping same-key requests (slow network, taps > 5 s apart), if the second succeeded and the first then failed, the branch showed the old-location text to a user at the new location — the NMAP-2 harm re-entering through the fallback. Fix (one line): the fallback re-reads `app.aiBriefingText` instead of using the tap-time capture. The review's fail-without-fix numbers were recomputed from the code with an exact Python port (scenario A new 0.0 m / old 8,915.3 m; scenario B new 11,054 m / old ≈ 0.0 m). Review verdict NOT CLEAN (0 Critical, 0 Major, 1 Minor, 1 Nit — `~/workspace/wildfire-fix-prep/fix22-review.md`); the Nit (`assertTrue(!...)` → `assertFalse(...)` in `RadarV2Test`) also addressed.

**Fix 22c** (first CI watch on PR #153, DO NOT MERGE — CodeRabbit Major 6016322271, review 6016322271, VALID, verified in code by the parent): the completion callback checked only `isAdded` and then displayed the briefing — if the user moved while the request was in flight, guidance for the OLD location displayed as current guidance. The failure fallback had the same gap (it compared the stored key against the *request-time* key, not the live location). Fix: the callback now reads the live location at completion; new pure `RadarV2.samePlaceForGuidance` (distance-based, 1 km threshold — the server builds guidance from reports within 25 km, so < 1 km is effectively the same guidance; GPS jitter is tens of metres; an exact-key recomparison would NOT work because LocationEngine publishes fresh fixes continuously and the doubles at completion almost always differ slightly even when the user never moved, suppressing nearly every slow response). Success path: on mismatch the result is still stored under its own (request-time) key, but the card shows the new `ai_moved` string ("تغيّر موقعك أثناء التحميل — أعد طلب الإحاطة لموقعك الحالي") instead of the briefing; the user re-taps at the new location. Fallback path: the stale text shows only when the stored key matches the request key AND the user is still (near) the request location. New `RadarV2Test` cases pin the predicate (same fix, ~555 m, ~2.2 km, null/one-sided, both null). Fragment wiring has no JVM seam — proven by the CI Android build + code review; no emulator/device run was performed in this VM. Instrumented check on the first watch: failed, but the job log (read via the signed-in browser, run 45107165979, job 137470981579) shows an SDK/emulator package install failure — sdkmanager "Error reading Zip content from a SeekableByteChannel" — the job died in 52 s before any emulator booted and no test ran; not the known boot timeout and not a code/test failure; non-blocking by causation. Re-watch on head dd2dc66: MERGE-READY — all 9 settled checks green/neutral, instrumented genuinely running (past the SDK-install phase), old Major verified satisfied in code; the "new" review 6016398864 is STALE (on the intermediate FIX_PLAN-only commit bead52b, pre-22c code — same finding, already fixed; its exact-equality suggestion would suppress nearly every slow response).

### Fix 22 — merged as PR #153 (merge c2ed73e, 2026-11-23 ~05:14 CET, parents f2e28f5 + dd2dc66, 8 files +205/−13)

**Fix 22d candidate (documented follow-up, NOT blocking — CodeRabbit Minor 4661699321, review 6016433307 on head dd2dc66, VALID, verified in code by the parent):** superseded briefing requests can replace newer guidance. Request A (location A) can still be pending when the user taps at B (the 5 s spacing only spaces starts; HTTP timeouts are 10 s + 10 s). If A completes after B showed its guidance, A's success branch writes A's text/key to the shared cache and shows `ai_moved` over B's guidance; A's failure branch shows `ai_failed` over B's guidance. This does NOT break the NMAP-2 guarantee — a late completion shows only status text, never A's guidance displayed as current at B; the cache overwrite only forces a refetch on the next tap at B (hits are key-scoped). Fix (small, well-defined): a request-ownership sequence guard — increment a counter per tap, capture it in the coroutine, ignore completions whose sequence is no longer latest, on both branches. Schedule as a later fix part.

### Fix 23 — require one native SOS countdown and distinct arrival fixes (NSOS-2 + NTEAMSV-1, both Major)

NSOS-2 (`SosFragment.kt`): every tap spawned its own 3-second countdown runnable, the field kept only the newest, so cancel stopped only that one and every surviving timer fired its own SOS. What changed since the report: Fix 18d already merged the offline double-tap (key reuse) and the server's 5-minute admission window already 409s a second distinct key online — so "two records" no longer happens. What still breaks, and only a fragment guard closes: (a) the mesh path is never deduped — each `sendSos` broadcasts a fresh mesh SOS echo; (b) the second POST's 409 is shown as a failure when the first SOS actually went out; (c) two runnables share one overlay — the first to finish hides it mid-countdown; (d) cancel still can't stop stale timers. Fix: a 3-line guard at the top of `startCountdown()` (`if (countdownRunnable != null) return`) + disable the button during the countdown (UX only — the snapshot collector can re-enable it mid-countdown, so the guard, not the disable, is the fix) + completion nulls the field only if still current. No `AppRepository` change — do NOT re-fix the key minting or the server window.

NTEAMSV-1 (`TeamLocationService.kt` + `TeamLocationLogic.kt`): the arrival streak counts beat *invocations*, not measurements — with a quiet GPS, two beats replay the same cached `Location` and satisfy the "two consecutive fixes" threshold. Fix: new pure predicate `TeamLocationLogic.isNewArrivalFix(fixTimeMs, lastCountedFixTimeMs)` (strictly-newer timestamp counts; equal/older neither advances nor resets) + an `arrivalLastFixTimeMs` atomic in the service, reset on mission change / non-`en_route` phase, checked distance-first then newness in `handleAutoArrival`. `nextArrivalStreak`/`shouldAutoArrive` and their test stay untouched. `fix.time` is already in hand — no new plumbing.

Decisions (all = the prep doc's recommendations): D1 fragment guard only (no repository-level `sending` guard — `doSend` is the only caller of `repository.sendSos`, and the offline case is already key-merged); D2 fragment-only, review + CI (no pure countdown-state holder — the race is in the Handler posting, which no JVM test can observe; NCAM-1 precedent); D3 new predicate + service marker (no signature change to `nextArrivalStreak`); D4 non-new fix neither advances nor resets (only an out-of-range fix resets); D5 server-side arrival distinctness protocol change OUT of scope as a follow-up (the flip's evidence fix IS the latest beat's fix, so a naive server check would reject legitimate flips — the server would need the last two distinct fix times per member); D6 web loop timestamp check OUT of scope (no finding covers it; the web loop requests a fresh position per beat).

Tests: `TeamLocationLogicTest` — predicate cases (first fix counts; replay/older don't; fresh does) + the replay sequence that fails against the old service shape (beat 2 with the same fix must leave the streak at 1) + out-of-range still resets. NSOS-2 has no JVM seam (fragment + Handler, same as NCAM-1) — review + CI build, stated honestly; no emulator/device run was performed in this VM.

Independent review verdict CLEAN (0 Critical, 0 Major, 0 Minor, 1 Nit — `~/workspace/wildfire-fix-prep/fix23-review.md`): NSOS-2 closed (guard first statement, single choke point, collector reasoning verified), NTEAMSV-1 closed (both marker resets present, distance-first order, flip path untouched), tests hand-verified (fail-without-fix confirmed by simulation). The one Nit is non-blocking and left as-is: the out-of-range assertion sits after the arrival assertion in the replay test rather than at beat 2 as prep §4.1 specified — same observable outcome, and the mid-chain reset is already pinned by the untouched existing test.

First CI watch on PR #154 (head 647ebef): MERGE-READY — all 10 check runs settled (9 green/neutral, instrumented failed with exactly the known boot-timeout annotation, non-blocking); build-and-test green incl. the new `TeamLocationLogicTest` cases; CodeRabbit review 6016612438 with 1 Minor + 1 Trivial nitpick. **Minor 4661856337 (VALID, verified in code by the parent, non-blocking):** compare arrival fixes by elapsed realtime, not `Location.time` — `NETWORK_PROVIDER` is requested alongside GPS and `onLocationChanged` does not filter by provider, so a backward clock correction after a counted network fix could make new fixes carry older `time` values and stall auto-arrival until the clock passes the marker. False negative only (arrival delayed in a narrow edge case), never a false arrival — it does not break the NTEAMSV-1 guarantee, and manual arrival still works. This also partially refutes the prep doc's R3 claim that "a device clock change cannot fake it" — true for GPS fixes, not for network fixes. Fix (small, well-defined): gate + marker on `fix.elapsedRealtimeNanos`, keep `fix.time` for the age check and heartbeat `fixTimeMs`, update the `isNewArrivalFix` doc comment — **23b candidate**, schedule as a later fix part. **Trivial nitpick (non-blocking, left as-is):** the replay sequence test duplicates the gate, so if the service lost its gate the test would still pass; suggests extracting a JVM-testable helper. Valid observation, but the test shape was explicitly specified in the approved prep doc §4.1, and service wiring is verified by code review + CI build (same honesty standard as NSOS-2's fragment wiring).
