### Fix 24 — keep command, team and roster views consistent through overlapping requests (CMD-1 + TEAM-1 + RSTR-1 + RSTR-2, all Major)

CMD-1 (`CentralCommand.tsx`): the SOS and locations loaders had no sequence guard (unlike the teams loader's ARC-W1), so a slow response could resurrect a just-resolved SOS. Fix: copy the teams loader's inline seq pattern into both fetchers — one ref per fetcher, checked after `apiFetch` AND after `res.json()`, gating data commits, error/fallback toasts, the `onRefresh()` call and the spinner clear. No `adminApi.ts` change (the util owns its own timeout signal and takes no signal param). **Extension beyond the prep doc (found by the test, not guessed):** the seq guard alone does NOT close the finding's exact interleaving — a fetch issued BEFORE the resolve can land AFTER it, and as the latest fetch its commit is not stale by the seq rule, so it reinstalls the resolved SOS until the next poll. `handleResolveSos` now bumps `sosFetchSeq` on a successful resolve, invalidating every in-flight SOS fetch; only a fetch issued after the resolve may commit, and the next 15 s poll refetches fresh. The prep's test case 2 (which waits for the second fetch to land before resolving) would have passed without this — the test as written resolves while the second fetch is still in flight, which is the true scenario.

TEAM-1 (`TeamPanel.tsx`): the manual flip and leave never checked the session after their await (unlike the resume/heartbeat F5 guards), so a late fatal/success could wipe a freshly joined session — the leave's teardown also calls `stopTeamTracking()`, which would kill a NEW session's FGS. Fix: one line each — `if (sessionRef.current !== current) return;` after the busy-flag reset, before any result handling. The F7 mission-seq check stays (it cannot cover the ok branch after a leave, which takes no sequence number); the busy-flag reset stays before the guard (the button must re-enable even when the verdict is dropped).

RSTR-1 + RSTR-2 (`RosterBoard.tsx`): `saveRoster` checked the date only before the PUT, then committed the response unconditionally — a save landing after navigation installed day A's roster under day B's header; `addPost` refetched the whole day after creating one post, silently restoring all unsaved edits. Fix: one shared `dateRef` mirror (a post-await check against the closed-over `date` state is a no-op — it always equals the requested date). Save discards a stale PUT response silently on both success and failure paths (the failure-path `fetchRoster()` is a second instance of the same bug — it would abort the new day's GET and install day A under B); `setSaving(false)` stays unconditional in `finally`. Add merges the POST's returned post locally (server already answers 201 + the created post) via a functional updater that re-checks `r.date === requestedDate`; the refetch survives only when there is no local roster to merge into, and never when the date moved.

Decisions (all = the prep doc's recommendations): D1 inline seq per fetcher (no shared hook); D2 silent discard of the stale save (no "saved day A" message under B's header); D3 no date-nav disabling while saving; D4 merge locally (no auto-save-first, no dirty flag); D5 object identity (matches the file's F5 doctrine); D6 the transient stale roster on navigation (old day visible until the new GET lands) is a recorded follow-up, out of scope; D7 render the real CommandMap first — **tried, failed in jsdom**: Leaflet's `L.map` effect throws `ResizeObserver is not defined`, and React 19 unmounts the whole tree without an error boundary. The test mocks CommandMap with a null stub; SosPanel (unmocked) still renders the SOS names the tests assert on. **Second D7 lesson: `vi.useFakeTimers()` does not flush the session probe's promise chain** (the probe rides `AbortSignal.timeout`, which the fake-timer clock never advances past) — the panel never unlocks under fake timers, so the tests use real timers throughout and drive the second fetch round with the visibility-return handler (`fireEvent(document, new Event("visibilitychange"))`) instead of waiting out the 15 s poll.

Tests (11 new/changed cases, all fail-without-fix verified by stashing the single component file): NEW `tests/components/central-command.test.tsx` (4: slow SOS fetch, the resolve-resurrection scenario, slow locations fetch + `onRefresh` gating, single-fetch control); `tests/components/team-panel.test.tsx` +3 (stale flip fatal, stale leave success, unchanged-session flip-fatal control — the existing leave control is not duplicated); `tests/roster-board.test.tsx` +4 (save-after-navigate, same-day save control, add-keeps-edits, add-no-edits control). **Test-harness note (RSTR-2 control):** the control must pass on the OLD code too — there the refetch is the only way the new post appears, so the GET mock is stateful (it returns the day with the new post appended only after the POST happened); a static GET mock made the control fail for the wrong reason.

Independent review verdict CLEAN (0 Critical, 0 Major, 0 Minor, 2 Nits — `~/workspace/wildfire-fix-prep/fix24-review.md`): CMD-1 closed (both loaders carry the ARC-W1 pattern; the resolve's seq bump is correctly placed inside `res.ok` before the local filter and genuinely closes the finding as written — a pre-resolve fetch is the *latest* fetch, so the seq guard alone cannot drop it), TEAM-1 closed (guard after the busy-flag reset, before any result handling, both handlers; cannot swallow a legitimate fatal — flip/leave render only when `!resuming`), RSTR-1/RSTR-2 closed (`dateRef` live; save guard covers both failure paths incl. their `fetchRoster()` calls; add merge uses the functional updater + `r.date` re-check), tests sound (visibility trigger is real code; CommandMap null-stub justified; RSTR-2 control's stateful GET mock correct and necessary), scope exact. **Nit 1 (addressed):** the resurrect test originally passed with the seq guard alone (microtask FIFO landed #2's commit before the resolve's filter), so it did not isolate the bump — #2's response is now gated and released only after the removal is asserted; verified: with the bump temporarily removed, only the resurrect test fails. **Nit 2 (addressed):** test count corrected 10 → 11 (4 + 3 + 4).

Local gates after the Nits: lint 0, build pass, server 875/875, React 1053/1053 (93 files).

**Fix 24b** (first CI watch on PR #155, DO NOT MERGE — all own checks green, build-and-test green incl. the 11 new tests; CodeRabbit review 6019281142, 3 comments, ALL verified VALID in code by the parent): **Major 4663970078** — a successful leave could skip native-tracking cleanup, a REGRESSION the TEAM-1 guard introduced: flip in flight → leave confirmed → flip returns fatal first, whose branch clears the session WITHOUT stopping tracking → leave then succeeds, but the guard returns early and `stopTeamTracking()` never runs; the only other call site is the manual stop button on the session card, which has just vanished — an orphaned FGS keeps collecting GPS + showing the notification after consent was withdrawn, indefinitely (no GPS fix → no beat ever sent → not a 60 s window). Fix (CodeRabbit's shape): in the leave's early-return branch, still call `stopTeamTracking()` when `sessionRef.current` is null (session gone entirely); a replacement session (non-null) keeps its own FGS. The stop runs even when the leave itself failed — the session is dead regardless of this POST's network result. New regression test pins it (flip fatal → leave success → exactly one `stopTeamTracking` call; fails without the guard-stop). **Minor 4663970060** — Add during a day load could lose the created post: the Add button was `disabled={saving}` only, so while day B's GET was in flight day A's roster stayed on screen with Add enabled; a POST for B completing before B's GET landed had its merge skipped (`r.date !== requestedDate`) and no refetch ran (`roster` non-null), and the GET then installed day B without the new post. Fix: `disabled={saving || loading}` — the same pattern the save button already uses. New test pins the disabled state during the gated load. **Minor 4663970069** — duplicate append: a GET issued before the POST but processed by the server after the POST's write can land first already containing the new post; the POST response then appended the same id again. Fix: the merge updater skips when `r.posts.some((p) => p.id === data.id)`. New test pins a single row. Fail-without-fix verified for all three (stashing the single component file fails exactly the new test(s)).

Follow-up (not a blocker, found while verifying the Major): the standalone flip-fatal orphan is PRE-EXISTING — the flip's fatal branch clears the session without stopping the FGS, so a flip that returns fatal with no leave in flight also leaves the service running with a dead token. Same harm family, one-line fix candidate (`stopTeamTracking()` in the fatal branch), schedule as a later fix part. The probe-fatal + flip race has the same shape (the resume probe clears the session without stopping tracking; a flip returning after that hits the guard and never stops it).

**Fix 24c** (second CI watch on PR #155, DO NOT MERGE — all own checks green, build-and-test green incl. the 14 Fix 24 + 24b tests; the three old comments verified satisfied in code; CodeRabbit new review 6019590100, 1 comment, VALID and BLOCKING): **Major 4664210880** — a pending same-day roster GET could replace a successful add. POST for day A in flight → operator navigates A→B→A → a fresh GET for A starts during the POST → POST completes and the merge appends the post → the GET (server read the day before the POST committed) lands after and `setRoster(data)` installs day A WITHOUT the post. The 24b Add-disabled fix blocks only the POST-starting-during-GET direction, not this GET-starting-during-POST one — and navigation is always allowed (D3). The harm is data loss, not display: `saveRoster` PUTs the whole `roster.posts` array, so a later save would delete the post server-side. A Fix 24 regression of the same class as the first watch's Major: pre-Fix-24 `addPost` called `fetchRoster()` after the POST, whose abort + new controller invalidated the pending GET as a side effect; the Fix 24 merge removed it. Fix: on the merge path, invalidate the pending read the way `fetchRoster` does — `rosterReqRef.current?.abort()` + replace the ref with `null` so its `isCurrent()` goes false — and `setLoading(false)` (the discarded read's `finally` only clears loading when it is current). Merely aborting is NOT enough: the ref would still point at the aborted controller, its `catch` branch would `setRoster(null)` + show a load error, wiping the merge. Safe by construction: on the merge path the date guard already passed, so any pending GET is for the same day, and the merged roster is the freshest state for it. New regression test pins it (POST gated → navigate A→B→A → release POST → release stale GET → post stays, no load error; fails without the invalidation).

**Fix 24d** (third CI watch on PR #155, DO NOT MERGE — all own checks green, build-and-test green incl. the 15 Fix 24 + 24b + 24c tests; the four older comments verified satisfied in code; CodeRabbit new review 6019868884, 1 comment, VALID and BLOCKING): **Major 4664428918** — the 24c invalidation ran unconditionally, but the merge updater rejects a live roster from another day. Add on A → navigate to B (B loads) → back to A (A's GET in flight) → POST completes: the date guard passes and the captured roster is A's, yet the LIVE roster is B's, so the merge is a no-op while the invalidation killed A's GET — the board is stuck showing B's roster under A's header with loading false and no fetch in flight (no effect refires: `date` is already A). Same shape when the live roster is null after a failed B load: an empty board, no error. Save is blocked by the existing `rosterDate !== date` check, so this is a stuck wrong-day display, not data loss. Fix: a NEW live roster ref (`rosterRef`, mirroring `dateRef`, assigned each render) gates the invalidation — when `rosterRef.current?.date !== requestedDate`, `addPost` refetches the selected day instead (the fresh GET reads post-commit and includes the new post, and `fetchRoster` aborts the stale GET itself); the local merge + 24c invalidation stay for a matching live roster, preserving unsaved edits. The check MUST use the live ref, not the closure's `roster` — the closure holds the click-time render and would say "matches" even when the live roster is B's. New regression test pins it (POST gated → B loads → back to A → release POST → fresh GET installs the day WITH the post, stale GET landing after is ignored; fails without the gate).

### Fix 25 — correct web radar inputs and evacuation guidance (RAD-1 + RAD-3 + WIL-1 + EVAC-1, all Major)

RAD-1 (`EvacuationRadar.tsx`): one good wind fetch stamped `isLive: true` forever; every later failure returned silently and the 10-minute interval retried into the void, while the display kept calling hours-old wind "Live", drawing the spread cone and writing directional guidance from it. Fix: the wind state now carries `fetchedAtMs` (the dead `isLive` literal is gone); a component-local `WIND_MAX_AGE_MS = 10 min` (one refresh cycle) bounds it; `windFresh` is derived once and gates EVERY wind consumer — drift heading, spread sector, wind arrow, value line and Live badge; all three failure paths (`!res.ok`, malformed body, `catch`) age the reading out once past the window and keep it inside the window (the SZM-2 doctrine from Fix 12). The derived check is belt-and-braces for backgrounded tabs, where the interval is throttled and the failure-path clearing alone would not run.

RAD-3 (`EvacuationRadar.tsx`): the blips memo filtered status, coordinates, confidence and distance only — never `timestamp`/`scanTime` — so a days-old pending report or satellite point within 30 km still determined `closestFire` and the escape heading, while the Home banner and wilaya severity excluded it. Fix: two filter lines reusing the shared `isFreshThreatTimestamp` (30 min / 2-min skew) — the radar, the banner and wilaya severity can no longer disagree on the same input, and the server comment at `wilayas.ts:37–41` ("ignored exactly as the client radar ignores them") is finally true. No clock tick in the component: the poll refreshes the props every 10 s (active) / 60 s (idle), so a threat aging past the window disappears at the next poll.

WIL-1 (`WilayaList.tsx` + `StatisticsPanel.tsx`): the flag `evacuationRecommended` means "a fresh critical citizen report exists" (derived in `deriveWilayaStatuses`, already freshness-gated server-side by W-M8) but the UI translated it into "active evacuation instructions" / "no evacuation orders", and the StatisticsPanel into "evacuations in progress" / "teams on alert" / "all regions under control". Fix is wording only — there is no official-order feed anywhere to switch to: WilayaList now says "بلاغات حرجة حديثة — يُنصح بالإخلاء / Évacuation conseillée (signalements récents)" and "لا بلاغات حرجة حديثة / Aucun signalement critique récent"; StatisticsPanel's three description branches now attribute everything to reports and recency. The red pulsing style on the true branch stays (it marks a critical-report wilaya, which is still true); the severity badges are report-severity labels, untouched. The WilayaList footer claiming coordination with civil protection is the same class of authority claim but outside WIL-1's letter — left as-is, recorded here as a follow-up.

EVAC-1 (`SafeEvacuation.tsx` + `src/utils/geo.ts`): the route-proximity check sampled every 4th vertex, so a fire exactly at the destination (or between sampled vertices) was never measured — the report's repro (`[[5,36],[5.01,36],[5.02,36],[5.03,36]]`, fire at `[5.03,36]`) reported 2.69875754966081 km for a true distance of zero and selected the green branch. Fix: new pure helper `minDistanceToPolylineKm(route: [number, number][], lat, lng)` in `geo.ts` (equirectangular point-to-segment, sub-metre error at these scales; empty → Infinity, single point → point distance, duplicate vertices → that point); the memo now measures every segment for every fire source, keeping its exact null contract; the intro comment now says "every road *segment* is checked". Per decision D3, `fireSources` is now also freshness-gated with the same two `isFreshThreatTimestamp` lines — its comment already claimed "same admission rule as the tactical radar", which is now true; the direction of harm there is false alarm only (a stale source can only shrink the proximity, never hide a fire). The native twin of this defect (NMAP-1, Fix 22) is the same geometry in Kotlin; no code is shared across the boundary.

Decisions (all = the prep doc's recommendations): D1 10-min wind window (= the refresh interval; a 5-min window would clear good wind mid-cycle on every transient failure); D2 keep the "Live" badge while age ≤ window (the window IS the freshness definition, same doctrine as the 30-min threat bar); D3 gate `fireSources` too (one line each, restores the asserted parity); D4 include StatisticsPanel (WIL-1's text names it; STA-1/2/3 in Fixes 35/36 are different defects, so nothing else would ever fix that wording) + the drafted ar/fr strings as written + the footer left alone; D5 helper in `src/utils/geo.ts` next to `haversineKm`, equirectangular (haversine cross-track would be exact spherical but more code for the same result to the metre at a 2.5 km threshold); D6 helper-only tests for EVAC-1 (the memo is a thin loop over the helper; no mounted SafeEvacuation test — the Leaflet map in jsdom is unproven and the helper test is the regression proof).

Tests (26 new cases, all fail-without-fix verified by stashing the single changed file): NEW `tests/components/evacuation-radar.test.tsx` (6: RAD-1 age-out past the window — badge flips to "غير متاح" + the no-wind guidance line + the value line; RAD-1 edge — a failure exactly at 10 min keeps the reading; RAD-3 stale report+satellite → empty state, fresh report → danger panel, +5-min future excluded, +1-min future included); NEW `tests/components/wilaya-list.test.tsx` (7: both WilayaList branches ar + fr, all three StatisticsPanel branches ar); `tests/geo.test.ts` +7 (`minDistanceToPolylineKm`: the report's repro verbatim → 0 with the old stride's 2.699 pinned by `haversineKm`, midpoint-of-segment → 0, far fire → >100 km, beside-road → ~1 km, single-point → point distance, duplicates → finite 0, empty → Infinity); `tests/client-threats.test.ts` +6 direct boundary tests for `isFreshThreatTimestamp` (30 min → true, 30 min + 1 ms → false, +2 min future → true, +2 min + 1 ms → false, unparseable → false, non-string → false) — the gate had no direct tests before, and RAD-3 now depends on it. **Fail-without-fix detail:** on old code 9 of the 12 component tests fail (the 3 that pass — the RAD-1 edge, the fresh report, the within-skew future — are controls that must pass on old code too) and all 7 helper tests fail; the 6 boundary tests pass on old code by design (they pin the pre-existing gate, which this part does not change). **Test-env lesson (Fix 24 D7, second instance): `waitFor` deadlocks under `vi.useFakeTimers()`** — its polling timer is faked and never fires, so all six radar tests timed out at 5 s. The RAD-1 tests now drive time with `act(async () => { await vi.advanceTimersByTimeAsync(...) })`, which flushes the fetch promise chain and React's scheduler; the RAD-3 tests assert synchronously (the blips memo renders from props, no async needed). **Test bug of my own, caught by the failure:** the first RAD-1 draft asserted the no-wind guidance line while rendering with no reports — that line only exists inside the `closestFire` branch, so the test now renders with a fresh report; the badge flip alone had already passed.

Local gates: lint 0, build pass, server 888/888 (82 files), React 1084/1084 (95 files).

Independent review verdict CLEAN (0 Critical, 0 Major, 0 Minor, 2 Nits — `~/workspace/wildfire-fix-prep/fix25-review.md`): all four findings closed (RAD-1's five consumers gated on `windFresh`, RAD-3's two filter lines, WIL-1's drafted strings verbatim in both files, EVAC-1's helper math hand-verified with the repro number independently confirmed — `haversineKm(36,5,36,5.03) = 2.6987575496608094` vs the report's `2.69875754966081`, helper returns exactly 0); the reviewer re-ran the new tests (12/12 jsdom, 44/44 node) and independently re-verified fail-without-fix (stashing `EvacuationRadar.tsx` fails exactly the 3 non-control radar tests; stashing `geo.ts` fails exactly the 7 helper tests). **Nit 1 (addressed):** the FIX_PLAN headline said "19 new cases" while its own itemization added to 25 (6+6+7+6) — corrected to 25, then to 26 when Nit 2's fix added one more test. **Nit 2 (addressed):** the French WIL-1 test rendered twice in one `it` without cleanup — split into two `it` blocks; the file now has 7 tests, all passing.

### Fix 25b — CodeRabbit follow-ups on PR #156 (Major 4665366918 + Minors 4665366914 + 4665366923)

First CI watch on PR #156 (head 27f4f66): all settled own checks green (build-and-test, lint-and-test, e2e, release, both Analyzes, CodeQL neutral, Vercel success), instrumented still in progress at the last poll; **DO NOT MERGE** on CodeRabbit review 6019281142-equivalent — 3 comments, all verified VALID in code at the head by the watch coordinator:

- **Major 4665366918 (EvacuationRadar.tsx:213) — wind state can stay "Live" past the 10-minute boundary while a refresh is pending.** `windFresh` was derived only at render and the only timer was the 10-minute fetch interval; a stalled fetch produces no render at the boundary, so nothing ages the reading out. The watch qualified the harm honestly: the parent poll re-renders at least every 60 s in every visible scenario, so the live overrun is bounded at ~60 s, not indefinite — but the component could not guarantee its own boundary and leaned on an incidental parent cadence. Fix: (a) the fetch carries `AbortSignal.timeout(30 s)` so a stalled request cannot pile up silently (the abort lands in the existing `catch`, which ages the reading out); (b) a successful fetch schedules its own one-shot expiry timer (`setTimeout(ageOutStaleWind, WIND_MAX_AGE_MS)`, tracked in a ref, cleared on cleanup and re-scheduled per success) — the reading now expires at its own boundary whether or not a render or a refresh happens first. The age-out guard makes a stale timer harmless: it clears only a reading actually past the window, so an older timer firing after a newer fetch cannot clear the newer reading.
- **Minor 4665366914 (EvacuationRadar.tsx:171) — the blips memo never recomputes as time advances.** The memo calls `isFreshThreatTimestamp` (which reads `Date.now()`) with deps `[reports, satellites, userLocation]` only — no clock dep — so a threat aging past 30 min stays on the radar until the next poll cycle (or forever, if polling has failed). Fix: a ticking `nowMs` state (`setInterval(..., 60 s)`, its own mount effect) added to the memo deps; `windFresh` now derives from the same `nowMs` clock instead of a bare `Date.now()`, so the badge and the memo agree on one clock. **Documented follow-up, CORRECTED by Fix 25c (CodeRabbit Minor 4665662282):** SafeEvacuation's `fireSources` memo has the identical no-clock-dep shape, and its staleness cuts BOTH ways — a threat aging OUT is false-alarm-only (a stale source can only shrink the proximity, never hide a fire), but a future-dated threat ENTERING the window is a false negative: a report more than 2 min in the future is excluded by the skew, and as time advances it becomes eligible yet the memo never recomputes, so route proximity can MISS the fire until the next data change. Clock-driven recomputation of `fireSources` is required follow-up work (the radar was the flagged surface; its props refresh on the same poll cadence in the normal case).
- **Minor 4665366923 (StatisticsPanel.tsx:105) — satellite-only wilaya status attributed to citizen reports.** `deriveWilayaStatuses` sets severity `"low"` from a fresh hotspot (confidence ≥ 80) while `activeFires` stays 0, so a satellite-only wilaya enters `mostThreatenedWilayas` and the non-critical branch said "recent reports below critical level" where no report exists — a genuine WIL-1 residue (Fix 25 reworded the branch to attribute the signal to reports, but for satellite-only wilayas that attribution is itself wrong). Fix: `threatenedHasReports = mostThreatenedWilayas.some((w) => w.activeFires > 0)`; the non-critical branch keeps the reports wording only when at least one threatened wilaya has real reports, and otherwise says "رصد أقمار اصطناعية حديث دون بلاغات حرجة / Détections satellites récentes sans signalement critique". The critical branch needs no split: `evacuationRecommended` is set only from a critical report (`server/routes/wilayas.ts:74`).

Tests (6 new cases in the 25b set): radar +3 (a stalled refresh that never renders still expires at its own boundary — the second fetch hangs forever; the fetch carries an `AbortSignal`; blips recompute with no prop change — a report ages out at 31 minutes via the 60 s clock tick) and StatisticsPanel +3 (satellite-only ar + fr; a mixed list keeps the reports wording). **Fail-without-fix (stash both src files): 5 of the 6 fail on old code; the mixed-list control passes on old code, as it must** (a mixed list shows the reports wording under both versions). **Test-env note:** the first full-gates run after 25b failed lint on the new signal test — `vi.fn(async () => ...)` types `mock.calls` as a zero-length tuple, so indexing `[1]` is a TS error (`TS2493`); the mock now declares its parameters (`(_url: string, _init?: RequestInit)`). Vitest alone never catches this (esbuild does not type-check) — the husky/lint gate is the backstop, same lesson as Fix 24's `Mock<() => void>`.

Local gates (25b): lint 0, build pass, server 888/888 (82 files), React 1089/1090 in-suite with the single failure proven a flake in isolation — run 1 hit the known dataset-outages timeout (file passes 17/17 in isolation); run 2 hit the known team-panel 50 ms timing flake ("shows the tracking card only when the bridge exists…", file passes 34/34 in isolation). Neither file is touched by Fix 25b; the lint error from run 1 (TS2493) was fixed and run 2's lint is clean.

### Fix 25c — CodeRabbit follow-ups on PR #156, second watch (Major 4665662295 + Minor 4665662282)

Second CI watch on PR #156 (head f9e92d1): all own checks that matter green (build-and-test, lint-and-test, e2e, release, both Analyzes, CodeQL neutral, Vercel success), instrumented failed with exactly the proven boot-timeout annotation (run 45171286266, job 137681180978, 1 annotation "Timeout waiting for emulator to boot." — the known non-blocking flake); **DO NOT MERGE** on CodeRabbit review 6021501632 (12:13:35Z) — 2 comments, both verified VALID in code at the head by the watch coordinator, and the three 25b findings verified SATISFIED IN CODE:

- **Major 4665662295 (EvacuationRadar.tsx:107) — schedule the wind expiry one millisecond past the window.** The timer fired at exactly `WIND_MAX_AGE_MS` after `fetchedAtMs`, but the age-out guard is a strict `>` and `windFresh` is `<=` — a timeout firing at precisely the boundary millisecond would be a no-op and the badge would stay live until the next 60 s clock tick. The watch checked CodeRabbit's evidence empirically and found one part WRONG: the claim that "the stalled-refresh test fails for the same reason" does not hold — the coordinator ran the test against the 25b head and it passed (real timers fire late by ≥1 ms, and the fake-timer advance executes the callback with the clock already past the boundary). So the live harm is a theoretical sub-millisecond window, not the 60 s overrun the comment implies — but the mechanism is real and the fix is one line: `setTimeout(ageOutStaleWind, WIND_MAX_AGE_MS + 1)`. Fix applied; the comment in code records the `>`/`<=` boundary reasoning. **New boundary test** pins it: the reading is still live at exactly 10 minutes and clears 1 ms later (hanging-refresh mock, so no refresh or tick can mask the timer) — verified fail-without-fix by temporarily reverting the `+ 1` (the test fails, the other 9 radar tests are untouched).
- **Minor 4665662282 (FIX_PLAN.md:1523) — correct the documented staleness direction for `fireSources`.** The 25b note claimed staleness there is "false-alarm-only (a stale source can only shrink the proximity, never hide a fire)". That holds for threats aging out, but not for future-dated threats entering the window: a report more than 2 min in the future is excluded by the skew, and as time advances it becomes eligible yet the memo never recomputes — so route proximity can MISS the fire until the next data change. A genuine false-negative direction the note omitted. Fix is a doc edit (applied above in the 25b section): both directions stated, clock-driven recomputation of `fireSources` listed as required follow-up work.

Local gates (25c): lint 0, build pass, server 888/888 (82 files), React 1091/1091 (95 files, zero failures).

### Fix 26 — refresh evacuation centres and restore management visibility (EVAC-2 + SZM-1, both Major)

- **Part:** Fix 26 (2 findings, both Major, all TypeScript/React + Express, no Kotlin). Prep: `~/workspace/wildfire-fix-prep/fix26-prep.md` (base main f2e28f5; the parent re-verified every citation against the post-25 main e1da6b9 — only `SafeEvacuation.tsx` moved between the two, by the Fix 25 EVAC-1/D3 changes; `SafeZonesManager.tsx`, `server/routes/safezones.ts`, `tests/safezones.test.ts` and `src/utils/live.ts` are byte-identical). Branch `fix/wildfire-26-evacuation-centres-refresh` from e1da6b9.
- **Shared root cause:** the server already knows when the centre list changes — every mutation clears its cache and broadcasts `safezones:changed` — but no client consumes that knowledge where it matters. The citizen page loads its private list once at mount and never looks again; the management page asks the one endpoint guaranteed never to return the records it needs to manage.
- **All 7 open decisions resolved to the prep doc's recommendations:** D1 event + 60 s polling backstop; D2 all three revalidation layers; D3 immediate refetch on the event (no 3 s debounce — the server cache was cleared before the broadcast, so the data is fresh on arrival); D4 separate `GET /api/safezones/admin` with `requireAdmin`; D5 manager live subscription yes; D6 manager `fetchZones` via `apiFetch`; D7 dedicated `zonesNotice` state (not `routeError`, which carries OSRM-failure text).
- **EVAC-2 (`src/components/SafeEvacuation.tsx`):** the one-shot load effect is extracted into a `loadZones` `useCallback` (same fetch, same `isActive !== false` client-side filter as defence in depth, same `ready`/`fallback` status) guarded by a sequence counter in a ref (the house ARC-W1 pattern — a stale run discards its result); the mount effect calls it once; `useLiveEvents` refetches immediately on `safezones:changed` (no new socket code); a 60 s `setInterval` backstop bounds staleness if the socket is dead (same interval as the server's zone TTL). A `[zones]` effect mirrors `zonesRef` and, when the active route's centre is no longer listed, clears `activeRoute` + `routeInfo` + `routeGeometry` and shows the dedicated `zonesNotice` (ar/fr). `handleCalculateRoute` revalidates against `zonesRef` twice — at the top (refuse a click on a dead zone, with the notice) and after the OSRM fetch, before `setActiveRoute` (the refresh-during-calculation hole: the `[zones]` effect cannot clear a route that was nulled at the top of the handler). A successful new calculation clears the notice. The OSRM call, the Fix 25 4-vertex proximity check, the Leaflet lifecycle and voice navigation are untouched.
- **SZM-1 (`server/routes/safezones.ts` + `src/components/admin/SafeZonesManager.tsx`):** new `GET /admin` (`requireAdmin`, reuses `loadZones()` — same 60 s TTL, same 5-min stale bound, same `X-Data-Stale` / `X-Data-Updated-At` headers, same 503) returning `zones.data` **unfiltered**. There is no `GET /:id` route in this router, so `/admin` cannot be shadowed. The public GET is unchanged — citizens never see inactive centres. The manager's `fetchZones` calls `apiFetch("/api/safezones/admin", "GET")` and routes a non-OK response through the `onAuthError` prop it already receives (the old plain-`fetch` version silently ignored non-OK responses); the mutations and the manual refresh button are unchanged. `useLiveEvents` → `fetchZones()` on `safezones:changed` keeps two admins' screens consistent. No UI change — the "inactive" filter, the dimmed inactive row and the reactivation toggle work as written once inactive records arrive.
- **Tests (11 new cases; fail-without-fix verified by stashing the 3 tracked src files):**
  - `tests/safezones-admin.test.ts` (new, 4 cases — a separate file from `tests/safezones.test.ts` because the router's module-level zone cache persists across tests in one file, and the existing DELETE test poisons it with an `undefined` snapshot): public GET excludes inactive records even with an admin token (passes on old code — pins the public contract); `GET /admin` with an admin token returns the full list; `GET /admin` without a token is 401 (a non-admin token cannot be minted — `generateAdminToken` only takes `AdminSessionRole = "admin" | "superadmin"`); PUT toggle to `isActive: false` hides the zone publicly but keeps it on `/admin`. On old code 3 fail / 1 passes (the public-filter case, as it must).
  - `tests/components/safe-evacuation.test.tsx` (new, 4 cases; real Leaflet works in jsdom — no mock needed): deactivated centre disappears + active route cleared + notice shown; refresh landing mid-calculation does not resurrect the dead centre (deferred OSRM); the 60 s backstop refetches with no live event (fake timers); control (no change → route calculates). On old code 3 fail / 1 passes (the control, as it must).
  - `tests/components/safe-zones-manager.test.tsx` (new, 3 cases): loads from the admin endpoint (never the public one) and shows the inactive row with its reactivation toggle; toggling an inactive zone PUTs `isActive: true` and refetches the admin list; a live `safezones:changed` event refetches. On old code 3 fail / 0 pass.
  - **Test-writing notes:** the `useLiveEvents` mock accumulates calls across tests in a file — capture the handler from the LAST call, not the first (an earlier test's handler belongs to an unmounted component; this cost one run on each file). The calculating label "جاري حساب المسار..." renders on BOTH zone buttons while calculating (activeRoute is null) — assert with `getAllByText` (length 2). After reactivating zone B, BOTH toggles read "نشط" — assert `getAllByRole("button", {name: "نشط"})` length 2 and `queryByText("مُعطّل")` null. The click-time `zonesRef` guard in `handleCalculateRoute` has no DOM-reachable test: the button disappears from the DOM the moment the list refreshes, so no click can land on a zone that `zonesRef` no longer lists — it is defense-in-depth, pinned by code review and by the post-fetch guard test, which exercises the identical check.
- **Local gates (Fix 26, before review fixes):** lint 0, build pass, server 892/892 (83 files), React 1102/1102 (98 files — the React config also re-runs the new server test file under jsdom, so the count is 1091 + 11, not 1091 + 7; the parent's first draft of this line said 1098/1098 and was corrected to the honest re-run outcome before commit).
- **Independent review: NOT CLEAN** (`~/workspace/wildfire-fix-prep/fix26-review.md`) — 0 Critical, 1 Major, 1 Minor, 3 Nits. **Major M1 (blocking):** a failed zone refresh wiped the active route and showed a false deactivation notice — the extracted `loadZones` kept the mount-only `setZones([])` in its `catch`, so one failed refresh (network blip, 500) emptied the list, the `[zones]` effect cleared the route, and the notice falsely claimed the centre was deactivated, with no restoration on the next successful poll. Fix: the `catch` clears only when `zonesRef.current.length === 0` (first load); the `fallback` status still shows the honest "couldn't verify" banner; the 200-with-`[]` branch stays (a genuine "no centres" statement). New regression test (route established → next zones fetch 500s → route survives, no deactivation notice, fallback banner shows), fail-without-fix verified by temporarily reverting the guard. **Minor m1:** `fetchZones`'s `[onAuthError]` dep re-introduced the ARC-M27 bug — AdminPanel's `handleAuthError` is a plain inline function with a new identity every parent render, so the mount effect refetched on every keystroke anywhere in the panel. Fix: the BadgeManager `onAuthErrorRef` mirror; `fetchZones` dep is now `[]`. **Nit N1:** the `[zones]` clearing effect now also clears `routeError` (the stale estimate warning lingered in the empty-state overlay). **Nit N2:** `capturedLiveHandler()` in both test files now throws a self-describing error when the component never subscribes (on old code it was a bare TypeError). **Nit N3 (observation, no change):** `zonesNotice` goes stale when the centre is reactivated — leaving it is defensible since the route stays cancelled.
- **Local gates (Fix 26, after review fixes):** lint 0, build pass, server 892/892 (83 files), React 1103/1103 (98 files).
- **Fix 26b — watch 1 on PR #157 (DO NOT MERGE, CodeRabbit review 6022612320, 4 comments, ALL VALID, all in the new Fix 26 code):**
  - **Major 4666538050** — `fetchZones` in SafeZonesManager had no sequence guard: the mount effect and the `safezones:changed` subscription can both start a GET, so an older response landing last overwrote the newer list and cleared `loading` while the newer request was still in flight (same defect class Fix 24 fixed in CentralCommand; Fix 26's own `loadZones` already had the guard). Fix: `fetchSeqRef` in `fetchZones` (seq captured at issue, checked after the await and in `finally`). New regression test with two overlapping GETs resolved out of order (older response discarded, refresh button stays disabled until the newer lands), fail-without-fix verified.
  - **Major 4666538069** — all three revalidation checks compared only `id`: PUT `/:id` validates the full zone schema (lat/lng included) and broadcasts `safezones:changed`, so an admin could move a centre keeping its id and the citizen page kept routing to the old destination (an in-flight calculation could also activate the captured old `zone` object after the refresh). Fix: new `zoneState(zone)` helper (`ok`/`gone`/`moved` — id AND coordinates) used at all three points ([zones] effect, click-time guard, post-OSRM guard), with moved-specific notices (ar/fr) at each point. New regression tests: moved centre clears the route with the moved notice; centre moved mid-calculation does not resurrect the old destination — both fail-without-fix verified. The click-time guard's moved branch shares the exact check with the post-OSRM guard, which IS tested (same untestability note as the gone branch).
  - **Major 4666538087** — the route-clearing branch never called `window.speechSynthesis?.cancel()` and never reset `isSpeaking`: the voice stop button lives inside the route-summary block and unmounts with the route, so a centre removed mid-speech kept playing with no way to stop it from this view (`onend`/`onerror` only reset on a natural end; the only other cancel is the component-unmount cleanup). The [zones] effect's own comment even claimed voice was cleared — it wasn't. Fix: `window.speechSynthesis?.cancel()` + `setIsSpeaking(false)` in the clearing branch (both gone and moved). New regression test with stubbed `speechSynthesis` + `SpeechSynthesisUtterance` (start the summary, deactivate mid-speech, assert cancel called once and the stop button gone), fail-without-fix verified.
  - **Minor 4666538099 (test-only)** — the PUT deactivation test pre-seeded zone-b as already inactive and never asserted `docUpdate` was called, so a PUT returning 200 without writing passed every assertion. Fix: assert `docUpdate` was called with `("safeZones", "zone-b", objectContaining({ isActive: false }))`. (Re-seeding zone-b active would not pin anything — the PUT reads through the module-level zone cache populated by the earlier GETs — so the write assertion is the real pin.)
  - **Test-stability fix (26b):** `calculateRouteForB`'s `findByText` timeout raised from the 1 s default to 5 s — the real Leaflet map init can push the route summary past 1 s under parallel load (two intermittent failures at ~1.1 s / ~2.1 s in targeted runs, both green on retry and both in the findBy, not the code). The assertion is unchanged. A full-suite run then showed the binding constraint is Vitest's 5 s default *test* timeout, not the findBy timeout (the speech test hit it at exactly 5000 ms) — the describe block carries a 15 s timeout now. That same full-suite run also hit the KNOWN dataset-outages flake (`tests/dataset-outages.test.ts:114`, 5 s timeout under load; 17/17 in isolation, as on Fixes 14/17/22b/25b) — re-run green, not a Fix 26 file.
  - **Local gates (Fix 26b):** lint 0, build pass, server 892/892 (83 files), React 1107/1107 (98 files).
- **Fix 26c — watch 2 on PR #157 (DO NOT MERGE, 1 NEW Minor 4666879313, VALID):** the 26b seq check in `fetchZones` ran after `apiFetch` resolves but BEFORE `res.json()` — if the older response's body streams slowly, a newer request can start and complete during the older `json()` await, then the older body overwrites the newer list (the exact defect Major 4666538050 was meant to close; the sibling `loadZones` in SafeEvacuation already checks after `res.json()` — verified by the parent — this was the one place that didn't; the 26b overlap test couldn't catch it because it resolves only pre-buffered bodies). Fix: one line — `if (seq !== fetchSeqRef.current) return;` after `await res.json()`, before `setZones(data)` (exactly CodeRabbit's suggested diff). New regression test with a deferred `json()` body (older headers arrive first, newer request completes, older body resolves last — asserts no overwrite), fail-without-fix verified by temporarily removing the line. Watch 2 also verified all 4 old comments SATISFIED IN CODE at head 8a0fa0d and all own checks green (instrumented still in_progress at last poll — non-blocking; the PR touches zero Kotlin files and build-and-test is green).
  - **Local gates (Fix 26c):** lint 0, build pass, server 892/892 (83 files), React 1108/1108 (98 files).

## Fix 27 — Keep web SOS recording and microphone lifecycle consistent (3 findings, all Major)

- **Part:** Fix 27 (triage row 58) — SOSM-1 + SOSM-2 + SOSM-3, all Major, all in `src/components/TrappedSOSModal.tsx`. Prep: `~/workspace/wildfire-fix-prep/fix27-prep.md` (base main `c2ed73e`; all citations re-verified at the post-26 main `ba430bd` — line numbers identical, file byte-identical to the prep base).
- **All 7 prep open decisions resolved to the prep doc's recommendations:** D1 step-gated effect with `step` in deps; D2 BOTH the `onstop` in `startRecording` AND the inactive-chunks branch in `stopRecordingAndSend`; D3 return 20 + a "recording complete" line; D4 ref-mirror the count, stop outside the updater; D5 `mountedRef` flipped by the unmount cleanup; D6 keep the close button live, make close win; D7 no change for post-recording GPS loss (the send-time guard stands).
- **Implementation:** SOSM-1 — the verification effect early-returns unless `step` is `verifying`/`no_location`/`no_fires`/`verified`, with `step` in the deps (re-runs are no-ops for post-recording steps; `nearestThreatState` freezes too; Escape suppression closes as a consequence). SOSM-2 — NEW shared `chunksToBase64` helper (same thresholds: blob > 200 bytes, base64 ≥ 100 chars); `onstop` installed in `startRecording` caches the auto-stopped take into the W-H2 `capturedAudioRef`; `stopRecordingAndSend` gains the inactive-but-chunks branch before the W-H2 check (closes the ms race and any browser-initiated stop); timer reworked — stop is a plain statement (no side effect in the updater), count mirrored in `recordingTimeRef`, display reaches 20, `recordingComplete` state drives the complete line (ar/fr) — set only when a recorder actually exists, so the permission-denied path keeps its amber warning as the honest message; `audioDuration` now reports 20 for a full take. SOSM-3 — NEW `mountedRef` (true on mount, false in the unmount cleanup); checked immediately after the `getUserMedia` await — a stream granted for a dead modal is stopped at once and the function returns before anything is stored, recorded, analysed, or timed.
- **Tests (7, NEW `tests/components/trapped-sos-modal.test.tsx` — no test referenced the modal before):** SOSM-1 recording survives a fresh GPS fix (new object identity) + success survives a fresh threat object + pre-recording verification control; SOSM-2 full 20 s take sent as real audio with duration 20 + ms-race case (recorder stopped externally while the onstop FileReader is pending → Send still sends the real take); SOSM-3 close during the permission await (stream stopped, recorder never constructed) + grant-while-mounted control. Mocks: `MediaRecorder` class mock, `FileReader` mock resolving asynchronously via `queueMicrotask` (deliberate — the race must be real), deferred `getUserMedia`, fetch routed by URL. Fail-without-fix verified by stashing the component: on old code all 5 finding tests fail and both controls pass. **Test-env lesson:** `vi.advanceTimersByTimeAsync` must be wrapped in `act` (or followed by an `act` flush) for React state updates to land before assertions; and a sync `act(() => {})` does NOT flush React's queued `setState` updaters — the first ms-race attempt passed vacuously on old code for that reason (the recorder was still "recording" at click time, so the manual path converted the audio); the final version stops the recorder externally, which isolates the branch.
- **Independent review: NOT CLEAN** (`~/workspace/wildfire-fix-prep/fix27-review.md`) — 0 Critical, 0 Major, 1 Minor (blocking), 1 Nit. **Minor 1:** `mountedRef` was never reset to `true` on mount — under StrictMode (the app runs under it, `main.tsx:68`) the mount → cleanup → remount simulation left the flag `false` for the component's lifetime, so the first `startRecording` in dev would discard the granted stream as "disposed". Fix: `mountedRef.current = true;` as the first line of the effect's setup body; the SOSM-3 control test now renders under `<StrictMode>` so CI pins the lifecycle — fail-without-fix verified (the control fails without the reset, passes with it). **Nit 1 (pre-existing, non-blocking, documented follow-up, NOT fixed here):** if `new MediaRecorder(...)` throws, the already-stored stream is never stopped on that path (the catch only sets `permission_denied`; tracks stop only at unmount) — bounded by modal lifetime, predates Fix 27.
  - **Local gates (Fix 27):** lint 0, build pass, server 892/892 (83 files), React 1115/1115 (99 files).
- **Fix 27b — watch 1 on PR #158 (DO NOT MERGE, CodeRabbit review 6023948226, 1 Minor + 1 Trivial, BOTH VALID):** **Minor (blocking):** `stop()` flips the recorder to `inactive` synchronously while the final dataavailable slice (~100 ms, `start(100)`) is still a queued task — the inactive branch in `stopRecordingAndSend` snapshotted the chunks immediately, so a Send landing in that window converted (and W-H2-cached) a take missing its last slice. Fix: NEW per-recording completion promise `recordingDoneRef` — minted in `startRecording`, resolved by the onstop handler with the converted audio (even when empty/short, so a waiter never hangs); the inactive branch awaits it when the recorder was stopped outside this call (`stoppedByThisCall` guard — the manual path overwrites onstop, so its promise would never resolve), raced against a 1 s timeout that falls back to the immediate snapshot (a recorder that never fires onstop must not hold Send hostage). **Trivial (non-blocking, folded in):** the `FileReader` mock ignored its blob argument and always resolved the canned string, so `audioUrl` equality could not catch a truncated conversion — the mock now records every received blob's size in `MockFileReader.blobSizes`. **Tests:** +1 regression (async-stop mock mode: `stop()` delivers the final 300-byte slice on a later microtask; Send tapped synchronously in between; the conversion feeding the POST must see 600 bytes) — fail-without-fix verified by temporarily removing the await (`expected 300 to be 600`); the 20 s test now also asserts `blobSizes[0] === 300`. **Local gates (27b):** lint 0, build pass, server 892/892, React 1116/1116 (99 files) — the first full React run showed the KNOWN dataset-outages timeout flake (`:114`, 5 s under load; 17/17 in isolation; file untouched).
- **Fix 27c — watch 2 on PR #158 (DO NOT MERGE, CodeRabbit review 6024343708, 2 Minors; watch-1 items both verified satisfied in code):** **Minor 1 (blocking, VALID):** the inactive branch's outer guard required `audioChunksRef.current.length > 0`, so a stop inside the first ~100 ms (before the first timeslice) skipped the `recordingDoneRef` wait entirely and the final slice — the ONLY audio — was discarded in favour of the siren. Fix: the length check moved from the outer guard to the snapshot condition only (where it skips a pointless FileReader call). **Minor 2 (VALID mechanism, NON-BLOCKING, recorded here as a deliberate trade-off, NOT redesigned):** on the 1 s race timeout the snapshot sends a partial take while the completed take only reaches the retry cache — but waiting unboundedly risks hanging Send forever if `onstop` never fires, and the siren fallback would discard ~20 s of real audio over a missing ≤100 ms slice; hitting the timeout needs a >1 s main-thread stall. **Tests:** +1 control (no `emitChunk()` before the deferred stop in async mode → the 300-byte final slice is sent as real audio, `blobSizes[0] === 300`) — fail-without-fix verified by temporarily restoring the old guard (`audioUrl` undefined — the siren path sends no audio). **Local gates (27c):** lint 0, build pass, server 892/892, React 1117/1117 (99 files), zero failures.

- **Fix 27 MERGED 2026-11-23 ~16:58 CET:** merge **1710753a043414e4370ad672abffe61ebfa01b4d** (parents ba430bd + a5d204a), PR #158, default merge message, branch NOT deleted. **Fix 27 complete: 3 findings. Tally: 73 merged, 206 remaining in 52 parts (Fixes 28–79); 27b/27c were CodeRabbit follow-ups (review Minor fixed the StrictMode lifecycle; 27b the timer-stop final-slice race + FileReader-mock Trivial; 27c the first-timeslice guard).**

## Fix 28 — Preserve web report drafts and release camera streams (3 findings, all Major)

- **Branch:** `fix/wildfire-28-report-drafts-camera` from main 1710753. **Prep:** `~/workspace/wildfire-fix-prep/fix28-prep.md` (base main 74706ca; parent re-verified all 8 cited files byte-identical between 74706ca and 1710753, so the prep's line numbers hold). **All 7 open decisions resolved to the prep doc's recommendations:** D1 widen `normalizeSubmissionResult` to `REPORT_STATUSES`; D2 request-owner token; D3 silent catch on an invalidated request; D4 single readwrite transaction; D5 incoming wins on tie, missing incoming `updatedAt` = epoch 0 (stale); D6 hand-rolled fake IndexedDB per the mesh-relay precedent; D7 keep the sync success message as-is.
- **RFSH-1** (`src/hooks/reportForm/reportFormShared.ts`): `normalizeSubmissionResult` accepted only `pending`/`verified`, so a durable replay of a moderator-resolved/rejected report was read as invalid and the offline sync loop threw and broke at the oldest draft, wedging the whole queue. Fix: the gate and the `SubmissionResultView.status` type both derive from `REPORT_STATUSES` (`pending`/`verified`/`rejected`/`resolved`) so they can never drift apart again. The badge gate (`useReportSubmit.ts`, `status === "verified"`) is a separate check and does not move.
- **RCAM-1** (`src/hooks/reportForm/useReportCamera.ts`): `startCamera` awaited `getUserMedia` with no mounted/request-owner check afterwards — a stream granted after Close/tab-switch was never stopped (camera live, no UI), and the camera button was bricked until unmount (`startCamera` early-returns on a truthy `stream`). Fix: NEW `cameraRequestRef` token, captured in `startCamera` right after the early return, bumped FIRST in `stopCamera` and by a mount-only unmount effect; after the `getUserMedia` await (and after the DOE await) a mismatched token stops the orphaned stream at once and returns without storing it; the catch path returns silently when invalidated (a deliberate Close must not produce a "camera unavailable" message).
- **DRAFT-2** (`src/utils/offlineDraftStore.ts`): `replaceOfflineDraftsUnlocked` read in a readonly transaction then wrote in a separate readwrite transaction — a tombstone written by another tab in between was overwritten, resurrecting the deleted draft; the live branch also overwrote unconditionally and restamped `now` with no freshness comparison. Fix: the read (`getAll`) and the puts now happen in ONE readwrite transaction (a readwrite transaction holds the store's write lock, so another tab's tombstone either commits before ours starts — we read it — or queues after ours — the delete happened later, correct); the live branch writes only when the incoming record is NOT stale (incoming `updatedAt` missing = epoch 0; equal stamps → incoming wins, as before). Tombstone branch, `stripStamp`-equal shortcut, tombstone pruning, legacy path, and `removeOfflineDraftsUnlocked` unchanged.
- **Tests (13 new, all fail-without-fix verified by stashing the single changed source file):** `tests/report-form-hooks.test.tsx` +8 — RFSH-1 pure contract (resolved + rejected replays accepted; pending/verified + malformed-shape + offline-draft controls) and the sync-loop regression (oldest draft replays `resolved`, newer draft `pending` → both removed, queue empty, success message; without the fix all 3 finding tests fail); RCAM-1 lifecycle (Close-during-permission → track stopped once, stream null, status closed; unmount-during-permission → stopped once; grant-while-mounted control; second tap after Close-during-permission works — not bricked; without the fix 3 fail, control passes). NEW `tests/offline-draft-store-race.test.ts` +5 — a hand-rolled fake IndexedDB (shared in-memory store, microtask request delivery, readwrite-transaction serialization, one-shot `afterRead` cross-tab hook writing the tombstone through the fake db directly, the way another tab would): tombstone-between-read-and-write survives (the incoming edit is NEWER than the stored one, so only atomicity saves it), tombstone-visible-in-read control, stale live write dropped, newer incoming wins + restamped, unchanged record keeps its stamp; without the fix the 2 finding tests fail and the 3 controls pass. **Fake lesson:** a transaction with zero requests still completes in real IndexedDB — the fake schedules an oncomplete check at construction, or a replace that puts nothing hangs (found during fail-without-fix: all 5 race tests timed out on old code before the fix).
- **Local gates (Fix 28):** lint 0, build pass, server 897/897 (84 files — the new race file runs in the server suite too), React 1130/1130 (100 files), zero failures.
- **Independent review:** CLEAN (`~/workspace/wildfire-fix-prep/fix28-review.md`) — 0 Critical/Major/Minor, 2 Nits, BOTH addressed (unused `deferredCamera.reject` removed; the `mediaDevices` stub now cleaned up in the describe's `afterEach`). The reviewer independently re-verified fail-without-fix per file and traced the race test's microtask ordering for both old and new code (the incoming edit is NEWER than the stored record, so only the transaction serialization — not freshness — saves it; deterministic).
- **Fix 28b — CodeRabbit review 6026765739 on PR #159 (watch 1: DO NOT MERGE), 3 Minors, all verified VALID by the parent against the branch code:** (1) `stopCamera` bumped the token but did not clear `cameraStartingRef`, so a retry tapped while the first permission prompt was still pending hit the guard and returned silently — the Fix 28 "not bricked" test only retried after the grant resolved. Fix: `stopCamera` clears the guard too (the pending request stays invalidated by the token; its `finally` re-clears harmlessly). (2) `persistDraft` kept the unstamped input in React state while the store restamped it, so the next sync's retry update (retry metadata, no `updatedAt`) read as stale (epoch 0) and was silently dropped while state showed it as saved. Fix: `replaceOfflineDrafts` RETURNS the records as committed and both call sites (`persistDraft`, the sync loop) hold the returned records; the stale branch hands the stored record back. (3) The batch `getAll` onsuccess catch rejected without `transaction.abort()`, so puts already queued could still commit while the caller followed the failure path. Fix: `abort()` before rejecting. **Tests:** +5 (camera retry-during-pending; store committed-return, stale-vs-fresh retry, mid-merge rollback — the fake gained abort rollback via a rows snapshot; hook committed-records wiring), all fail-without-fix verified per file (camera 1 fails, store 3 fail, queue 1 fails, all other tests pass). The abort test first passed vacuously on old code (the d1 change was stale, so no put was ever queued) — fixed by seeding directly with old stamps. The mocked `replaceOfflineDrafts` in the hook tests now echoes its input (the store's contract for an uncontended write) instead of resolving `undefined`.
- **Fix 28c — CodeRabbit review 6027189101 on PR #159 (watch 2: DO NOT MERGE), 1 new Minor, verified VALID by the parent:** the old three Minors were not re-flagged. The new one: start A, Close, start B before A completes — A's unconditional `finally` cleared `cameraStartingRef` while B was pending, so a third tap started C and invalidated B (a follow-on of 28b's guard reset — the parent had wrongly called the unconditional `finally` harmless). Fix: the `finally` clears the guard only when `myRequest === cameraRequestRef.current`; `stopCamera`'s immediate clear stays. **Tests:** +1 (stale completion does not clear the retry's guard — third tap hits the guard, B owns the camera), fail-without-fix verified (fails on the old camera file, 35 other tests pass).
- **Local gates (Fix 28c):** lint 0, build pass, server 900/900 (84 files), React 1136/1136 (100 files), zero failures.
- **Fix 28 MERGED 2026-11-23 ~19:59 CET:** merge **78fe14685d6f87281cab8adbf17829984b38edde** (parents 1710753 + effae54), PR #159, default merge message, branch NOT deleted. **Fix 28 complete: 3 findings. Tally: 76 merged, 203 remaining in 51 parts (Fixes 29–79); 28b fixed the watch-1 CodeRabbit Minors (camera retry guard, committed-records return, transaction abort) and 28c the watch-2 Minor (stale completion guard); watch 3 was MERGE-READY (all head checks green; instrumented failed but fails identically on untouched base main 1710753 — pre-existing, unrelated).**

## Fix 29 — Restore web feed polling, session state and volunteer validation

- **Findings (3, all Major):** POLL-1 (`useObservatoryPoll.ts` — cleanup-only mount effect left `mountedRef` false after a StrictMode replay, so all dataset commits/loading writes were discarded while the poll kept fetching); AUTH-1 (`useAuth.ts` + `lib/session.ts` — every `useStaffSession()` owned private state + its own fetch, so App/RosterBoard/StaffManager disagreed after login); VOL-1 (`VolunteerRegistration.tsx` + `utils/phone.ts` — client validated the cleaned phone but sent the raw one; an 8-digit Tunisian number passed the client and died on the server's `min(10)`).
- **Decisions (all to prep recommendations):** D1 setup-arm restore; D2 module store in session.ts; D3 keep `subscribeSessionPoll` generic; D4 reset cache on last staff unsubscribe; D5 client-side E.164 normalization; D6 volunteer form only; D7 keep typed text, send normalized.
- **POLL-1:** the mount effect gained a setup arm (`mountedRef.current = true;` + cleanup returning false/abort/null). Nothing else in the hook touched.
- **AUTH-1:** `src/lib/session.ts` fully rewritten — `StaffSession` type moved here; module store (`sharedSession`, `sharedLoading`, `sharedFetch` in-flight dedup, `sessionSubscribers`); `fetchSharedSession()` (one fetch, same response mapping as the old refetch, never throws, notifies subscribers); `subscribeStaffSession(cb)` (immediate snapshot, reset to unauthenticated+loading on last staff unsubscribe); `getStaffSessionSnapshot()` / `isStaffSessionLoading()`; `tick()` calls `fetchSharedSession()` once when staff subscribers exist AND still calls generic listeners per tick; interval lives while generic listeners > 0 OR staff subscribers > 0. `useAuth.ts` is now a thin subscriber (same return shape; re-exports `StaffSession`). `useSessionProbe.ts` verified first — its own `/api/admin/session` probe rides `subscribeSessionPoll` unchanged.
- **VOL-1:** `normalizeMaghrebPhone(value)` added to `src/utils/phone.ts` (strip `[\s\-]`, then E.164 per national form; already-prefixed passes through; no-match returns unchanged — validation rejects it). The form validates AND sends the normalized string. Two deliberate readings of the prep, recorded for the reviewer: (1) the prep's §3 mapping table governs — every national number becomes E.164, so `0550 123 456` → `+213550123456` (the §4 shorthand `→ 0550123456` is the stripped intermediate, not the output); (2) the national shapes collide (DZ, MA, LY all accept `0[567]XXXXXXXX`), so first match wins in table order, DZ first — an MA national number therefore normalizes DZ-first (still valid on the wire); a prefixed `+212` passes through unchanged. Server schema unchanged (backstop), `isValidOptionalPhone`/ReportForm untouched (Fix 39).
- **Tests (25 new):** POLL-1 — 2 in `tests/observatory-hooks.test.tsx` (StrictMode replay commits all five + loading clears; no-StrictMode control). AUTH-1 — 5 in NEW `tests/staff-session-shared.test.tsx` (refetch from A authenticates B without a tick; exactly one `/api/auth/session` fetch per 60 s tick with 3 instances; late mount reads current session at once; two simultaneous refetches share one in-flight request — added for review Nit 1; `useSessionProbe` regression guard). VOL-1 — 14 in NEW `tests/phone.test.ts` (pure table incl. both repros, all four prefixed passthroughs, national forms, invalid unchanged) + 3 in NEW `tests/components/volunteer-registration.test.tsx` (POST body carries the normalized phone for both repros; invalid phone → no fetch + error) + 1 server pin in `tests/volunteers.test.ts` (spaced phone still 400).
- **Fail-without-fix (all proven by stash):** POLL-1 — StrictMode test FAILS / control passes on old code; AUTH-1 — 3/4 fail (probe guard passes — correct, it is a regression guard); VOL-1 helper — 14/14 fail; VOL-1 component — 2/3 fail (invalid-phone control passes).
- **Test-environment finding (NOT a Fix 29 code change — follow-up):** a `wrapper` component that renders `<StrictMode>` does NOT double-invoke effects in this suite (probed 2026-11-23: setups=1 via wrapper, setups=2 via `reactStrictMode: true` and via direct JSX). The POLL-1 test therefore uses `renderHook(..., { reactStrictMode: true })`. Consequence: the Fix 27 `trapped-sos-modal` StrictMode guard (wrapper pattern) is vacuous — it never exercises the replay. A future part should convert it to `reactStrictMode: true` and re-verify it still passes. Also: `waitFor` deadlocks under fake timers — the AUTH-1 timer tests drive the clock with `act` + `advanceTimersByTimeAsync` and assert after each flush.
- **Fix 29b (CodeRabbit review 6028297096 on PR #160, 4 Minors, all verified VALID by the CI watch + independently by the parent):** (1) post-login refetch could reuse a pre-login tick probe — `fetchSharedSession(force)` + generation guard: only the latest started fetch writes state; a force bypasses a tick probe but shares a forced in-flight one (two simultaneous refetches still cost one request); `useAuth` refetch is now forced. (2) visibility handler never installed for staff-only mounts — `subscribeStaffSession` now calls `hookVisibilityOnce()` (idempotent). (3) no initial probe per subscriber family — first staff subscriber fetches at once (App.tsx mounts the admin probe at line 31 before the staff hook at line 32, so the staff session waited up to 60 s with a valid cookie — live startup regression); late generic listeners get an immediate `listener()` call too (first mount already ticks, so no double probe). (4) spaced international numbers rejected — the normalizer's fallback now returns the stripped candidate `v`, not the raw input. Tests: +3 in `tests/staff-session-shared.test.tsx` (probe-first startup; staff-only visibility recovery; forced refetch vs stale in-flight probe with a deferred fetch mock) + 5 cases in `tests/phone.test.ts` (spaced/hyphenated international). Fail-without-fix proven by stash: exactly the 3 new staff tests fail on old code, 5/5 others pass; 4 of the 5 new phone cases fail (the ` 0550123456 ` case already passed — its branch matches the stripped form). Test-ordering lesson recorded in the test file: the visibility test must run before any probe-mounting test and the staleness test last, or module-level handler/fetch state leaks make them pass vacuously / wedge later tests. Follow-up (not a blocker): a hung session fetch wedges `sharedFetch` forever — consider `AbortSignal.timeout` on the session fetch in a later part.
- **Fix 29c (CodeRabbit review 6028708124 on PR #160 head b933fda, 1 NEW Minor, verified VALID):** the generation check sat BEFORE `await res.json()` — a forced fetch could start and commit while an older body was still parsing, then the older parse resolved and overwrote the store with the stale session (current subscribers kept the newer one in React state, but a late subscriber read the stale one). Fix: re-check the generation AFTER `res.json()` and before assigning `sharedSession`. Test: +1 in `tests/staff-session-shared.test.tsx` (stale body parsing late; the first fetch's `json()` is deferred until after the forced refetch commits; the late subscriber must still read commander). Test-design lesson: the refetch must be called only AFTER a flush lets fetch #1 pass the pre-parse check and park inside `res.json()` — calling it synchronously lets #1 bail at the pre-parse check and the race is never exercised (the first version of this test passed vacuously on old code for exactly that reason; caught by the stash proof, fixed, re-proven). Fail-without-fix proven by stash: exactly the 29c test fails on 29b code, 8/8 others pass.
- **Local gates (Fix 29 + 29b + 29c):** lint 0, build pass, server 920/920 (85 files; baseline 900 + 20 new), React 1170/1170 (103 files; baseline 1136 + 34 new) — clean full run, no flakes this time. e2e not runnable in this VM (Firebase emulator needs Java) — CI covers it.
- **Independent review: MERGE-READY** (head 78fe1468, working tree as reviewed). All 3 Majors verified closed by reading the code; both contested prep readings judged the right calls; no test vacuous; no scope creep; no type-safety hole. 1 Minor (review Minor 1): Moroccan/Libyan `05/06/07` national numbers normalize DZ-first — documented limitation, does not reintroduce VOL-1; follow-up = optional country hint fed from the selected wilaya's country, natural place Fix 39. 1 Nit (concurrent-refetch dedup untested) — ADDRESSED: new test "two simultaneous refetches share one in-flight request", passes 5/5 in its file.

## Fix 30 — General web controls and accessibility (AI, HDR, DLG, TST)

- **Branch:** `fix/wildfire-30-web-controls-a11y` from main **47f123d** (PR #160 merge). Prep: `~/workspace/wildfire-fix-prep/fix30-prep.md` (base e1da6b9; all 5 in-scope files byte-identical between e1da6b9 and 47f123d, so prep line numbers held). All 6 open decisions D1–D6 resolved to the prep's recommendations. Wilaya codes confirmed as the standard administrative numbers (El Tarf 36, Skikda 21, Annaba 23, Souk Ahras 41, Jijel 18, Guelma 24).
- **Findings (all Minor):** **AI-1** (`AICopilot.tsx`) — a context change inside the 5 s throttle window stranded the spinner (the throttled replacement minted an ID, so the aborted predecessor's `finally` refused to clear `loading`) and skipped the load entirely. **AI-2** (`AICopilot.tsx` + `maghrebRegions.ts`) — the wilaya selection was the rendered display string, so a language switch left the old-language string in state/request with no chip highlighted. **HDR-1** (`HeaderBar.tsx`) — any past backend contact read as present reachability indefinitely. **DLG-1** (`ConfirmDialog.tsx`) — the dialog had no accessible name. **TST-1** (`ToastStack.tsx`) — toasts were never announced to screen readers. AICopilot is DORMANT (zero imports; sidebar mount removed by owner decision) — fixed correctly anyway, NOT re-mounted, NOT deleted (D6).
- **Implemented:** AI-1 — the request ID is minted only after the cache + throttle checks, and a throttled call schedules a deferred `fetchGuidance()` (force=false) after the remaining interval in a new `deferredTimeoutRef` (cleared in the effect cleanup, cleared-and-replaced on re-schedule, cleared when a real run or a cache hit resolves the context). AI-2 — `NORTHEAST_ALGERIA_FOCUS` entries gained a stable `code` (string); state stores the code; the request label is derived from the code at send time in the current language (wire behavior unchanged); the cache key now embeds the code. HDR-1 — `backendReachable = lastBackendContact > 0 && nowTick - lastBackendContact <= STALE_AFTER_MS && sync !== "offline"` (the "اتصال:" timestamp is untouched — it is the honest historical record). DLG-1 — `aria-labelledby="confirm-dialog-title"` on the dialog + `id` on the heading (one static id is safe: only one dialog is ever open). TST-1 — `role="status"` + `aria-live="polite"` on the toast container; NO focus change (moving focus to a transient auto-dismissing toast is the wrong fix — recorded so a reviewer does not re-raise it).
- **Tests (17 new):** NEW `tests/components/ai-copilot.test.tsx` (5: throttle-window spinner + deferred load; StrictMode double-mount; completed answer replaced by the deferred load; language switch keeps the selection + French name on the wire; cache-key stability control) — the fetch mock rejects with AbortError on abort, like a real fetch (a signal-ignoring mock never lets the predecessor's finally run, so the spinner test would fail on fixed code too). NEW `tests/components/header-bar.test.tsx` (7: stale contact, offline-in-window, fresh-contact control, exact-3-min boundary, 3-min+1-ms boundary, dynamic flip via the 30 s nowTick, timestamp control) — ResizeObserver stubbed, clock frozen. NEW `tests/components/confirm-dialog.test.tsx` (2: named by title + aria-labelledby; closed renders nothing). NEW `tests/components/toast-stack.test.tsx` (3: polite status region, second toast inside the same region, empty renders the empty region — corrected by 30b, was "empty renders nothing").
- **Fail-without-fix (per-file stash, all proven):** AICopilot stash → 4/5 fail (3 AI-1 + AI-2 chip; the cache control passes — correct). HeaderBar stash → 4/7 fail (stale, offline, boundary+1ms, flip; 3 controls pass). ConfirmDialog stash → 1/2 fails. ToastStack stash → 2/3 fail. maghrebRegions stash → AI-2 chip test fails (via the wire assertion).
- **Fix 30b (CodeRabbit review 6029588769 on PR #161, 1 Major, verified VALID):** the live region was unmounted when the toast list was empty (`if (toasts.length === 0) return null;`), so every toast arrived in the same commit as the region itself and screen readers can miss content that appears together with its live region (W3C ARIA22) — and since toasts auto-dismiss in 4 s, the "first toast after an empty interval" is essentially every toast, so the TST-1 fix was partly defeated by its own early return. Fix: the container always renders (possibly empty — zero children, zero height, nothing visible). Tests: the empty-list test now requires the empty region (was: asserts nothing renders), +1 test for empty → nonempty rerender asserting the SAME region element receives the toast.
- **Local gates (Fix 30 + 30b):** lint 0, build pass, server 920/920 (85 files), React 1187/1188 in the full run (107 files) — the single failure is the Fix 26 safe-evacuation Leaflet-init timing flake (untouched by 30/30b), which passes 8/8 in isolation; all 18 new tests pass. e2e not runnable in this VM (Firebase emulator needs Java) — CI covers it.
