## Fix 43c — CodeRabbit round 2 on PR #174 (2 Minors, both verified in code)

- **Watch 2 (head 3a6504e7): DO NOT MERGE** — all own checks green (lint-and-test, build-and-test, e2e, release, Analyze ×2; CodeQL neutral; instrumented in progress — known pre-existing non-blocker); the 4 old 43b Minors verified FIXED in code; mergeable true. **CodeRabbit review 6040115063 (2026-11-24T13:33:59Z): 2 NEW actionable Minors, both verified VALID by the watcher AND by the parent in code at head.**
- **Minor 1 (inline 4680724112, `server/routes/users.ts:181`)** — the sweep's `catch` logs the failure but leaves `rosterCleanup` at the default all-zeros `{days: 0, removed: 0, failed: 0}` — indistinguishable from a sweep that finished with no affected days. Same misleading-shape class 43b fixed for the null-read branch; the throw path (`collectionGet` throws, or a `docSet` inside `Promise.all` throws) was missed. Account deletion still completes; only the report is wrong.
- **Minor 2 (inline 4680724141, `src/components/CentralCommand.tsx:294`)** — the 43b upsert replaces by `teamId` alone, but `teamId` does not identify a dispatch: the server appends a second entry on clear + re-dispatch (nothing ever removes entries; the Fix 43 "genuine re-dispatch after clear" test expects `"ok"` + 2 rows), and `applyLocalDispatch` runs on ANY `res.ok`. Consequences, both real: a fresh dispatch after clear loses D1 locally (server [D1, D2], client [D2]); a replay of D2 when local already holds [D1, D2] replaces BOTH with D2 ([D2, D2]), destroying D1 locally until the next poll.
- **Implemented (2 source files):** users.ts — the catch now sets `rosterCleanup = { ...rosterCleanup, incomplete: true }` (spread preserves any partial days/removed/failed counts if the throw lands mid-sweep) and the warn message now says "sweep incomplete" (it previously said only "failed"). CentralCommand.tsx — `applyLocalDispatch` reconciles by stable identity (`teamId` + `dispatchedAt`): an entry with the same teamId AND dispatchedAt is replaced, anything else appends. A fresh dispatch (new dispatchedAt) appends; a replay (same dispatchedAt) replaces only its own entry. An item without teamId or dispatchedAt (the raw request-body fallback) still appends. Edge case, accepted: two dispatches for the same team in the same millisecond would share an identity and the second would replace the first locally until the next poll (server-generated ISO millisecond timestamps make this vanishingly unlikely).
- **Tests (+3 new; 1 updated):** USR-2 thrown-sweep +1 (`tests/users-security.test.ts` — `collectionGet` rejects → DELETE 200 + `rosterCleanup: {days: 0, removed: 0, failed: 0, incomplete: true}` + warn with the agentId and "incomplete"). Fresh dispatch +1 (`tests/components/central-command.test.tsx` — SOS with D1 (10:00, "first"); POST returns a fresh D2 (11:00, "second", `alreadyDispatched: false`) → local [D1, D2], D1 intact). Replay +1 (SOS with [D1, D2]; POST returns the stored D2 verbatim → local stays [D1, D2], D1 intact). **UPDATED 43b test:** its mocked replay used `dispatchedAt: 11:00` while the stored entry had 10:00 — no real replay ever produces that (the server returns the stored entry verbatim); with identity reconciliation it would append instead of replacing. The mock now returns the stored entry verbatim (10:00, notes "second" — the local copy is stale on notes only). **Test incident (parent-caught):** the replay test first asserted the final state inside `waitFor` — which exits immediately because the PRE-dispatch state already satisfies it ([D1, D2]) — and passed vacuously on old code (a debug dump of the recorded history showed the corrupted `["second","second"]` render last). Rewritten: wait for the dispatch POST to be issued, flush the response chain with `act` + a zero-timeout, then assert over the recorded `sosCalls` history that no `["second","second"]` state was ever rendered, plus the final state. The history assertion is what survives a later poll restoring [D1, D2].
- **Fail-without-fix (proven):** stashing the 2 source files (`server/routes/users.ts`, `src/components/CentralCommand.tsx`) fails exactly the 3 new tests (thrown sweep, fresh dispatch, replay); all other targeted tests pass (20/21 server, 13/15 React). The updated 43b test passes on 43b code by design (it discriminates pre-43b code only).

- **Fix 43c follow-ups:** none new. The 43b follow-up (durable `dispatchedTeams` accumulation) stands.

- **Findings:**
  - **DB-2 (DORMANT)** — legacy persistence helpers bypass the privacy/idempotency lifecycle: `saveReportToFirestore` writes the whole report into the public doc with no privacy split; `deleteReportFromFirestore` swallows shard failures in the admin branch and omits the shard deletes entirely in the client branch, and never touches `reportIdempotency`. Zero runtime callsites for save; delete imported unused in `server/routes/admin.ts:9` (the delete route uses `purgeReportWithIdempotency`).
  - **D2 SCOPE EXPANSION (named, not silent):** the LIVE purge path has the same shard omission on its client branch — `purgeReportWithIdempotency`'s admin branch deletes private+image shards in the batch but its client branch deletes only the report doc + idempotency keys.
  - **DVC-1 (LATENT)** — device-binding cookie splits at the FIRST dot (`server/deviceBinding.ts:37`); a dotted id round-trips to null. The base64url signature provably contains no dots, so only the id half can. Latent because the only production issuer (POST `/enroll`) mints `web_<hex>` or accepts only `/^web_[A-Za-z0-9_-]+$/`.
  - **MAIL-2 (LIVE)** — no deadline on any of the three provider fetches (`server/email.ts:24/34/50`, all inside `sendEmail`); a stalled fetch holds subscribe responses and all later alert batches indefinitely.
  - **MAIL-3 (LIVE)** — `sendVerificationEmail` silently no-ops with no provider (`email.ts:188–189`) while all THREE subscribe sites answer "Check email to verify" (`notifications.ts:403/441/452`).
  - **FS-3 (LIVE)** — resolve invalidates only the `teamMissions` collection, never each mission doc (`resolveSosAtomically`, `server/fs.ts:876` admin, `:908` client); `docGet` serves the cached `en_route`/`on_scene` copy for up to 60 s.
- **Implemented (7 source files):** db.ts — `saveReportToFirestore` + `deleteReportFromFirestore` DELETED (2-line comments left at the deletion sites pointing at the replacements) + the 2 shard `batch.delete` lines added to the purge CLIENT branch (mirroring the admin branch, with the S-H1 comment); deviceBinding.ts — `indexOf(".")` → `lastIndexOf(".")` (the `idx <= 0` guard stays); email.ts — `EMAIL_PROVIDER_TIMEOUT_MS = 10_000` + `signal: AbortSignal.timeout(...)` on all three fetches, `sendVerificationEmail` now returns `Promise<"sent" | "not-configured">` (`"not-configured"` on no provider, `"sent"` after `sendEmail`); notifications.ts — all three subscribe sites branch on the return: not-configured → 200 + additive `emailDelivery: "disabled"` + message `"Subscription saved, but email delivery is not configured — you will not receive a verification email."`, record still persisted unverified; fs.ts — per-doc `invalidateDocCache("teamMissions", id)` for each cleared mission in BOTH resolve branches, inside the existing `resolved` guard, collection invalidation KEPT; admin.ts — the unused `deleteReportFromFirestore` import removed. **FS-3 mechanism (parent decision, prep D7 silent on the mechanism):** the cleared ids are collected in a closure array declared outside each transaction callback and invalidated after the commit — NOT returned in `SosResolveResult`, because the resolved outcome is asserted with exact `toEqual` in FIVE places (atomicity.test.ts:584, sos-resolution-availability.test.ts:179/:218/:230/:236); a type change would have meant 5 assertion updates for an internal cache detail. Invalidation is idempotent, so a retried transaction pushing duplicate ids is harmless, and the `resolved` guard means a failed transaction invalidates nothing.

Fix (D1/D2): **DELETE the feature** (Fix 38 API-2 / Fix 42 RPT-3 deletion precedent): `server/swagger.ts` (whole file); `server/server.ts:11` (swaggerUi import), `:15` (swaggerSpec import), `:136` (mount), `:200` (boot log line); `tests/e2e/smoke.spec.ts:82–85` (the swagger smoke test — asserts only that the UI shell loads, so it never guarded the finding); `playwright.config.ts:31` (`ENABLE_SWAGGER: "true"`); `scripts/screenshot.mjs:27` (`ENABLE_SWAGGER: "false"`); deps via `npm uninstall swagger-jsdoc swagger-ui-express @types/swagger-jsdoc @types/swagger-ui-express` + review the lock diff; README's 4 claims (:109 stack-table row, :150 file-tree line, :250–253 section, :344 + :360 checklist lines). Post-edit grep for `swagger|Swagger|api-docs|swaggerSpec|swaggerUi|ENABLE_SWAGGER` must return zero code references. **TRG-2 (LIVE)** — `snapshotIfDue` (`server/teamRegistry.ts:128–141`) sets `lastSnapshotAt` BEFORE the write (:132) and discards the resolved boolean; the `.catch` (:140) is provably **DEAD** — `docMergeTeamPositionIfActive` (`server/fs.ts:123–170`) catches everything internally and returns `false` on three paths: no DB (:137, silent), skip — member gone/inactive/wrong team/wrong tokenGen (:143–145/:154–156, silent, by design), transaction failure (:166–169, fs-level warn). The skip case is a narrow race (the heartbeat handler validates membership at `teams.ts:518–543` before the snapshot call at :558; both removal paths call `removeMember` at :651/:930), so treating ANY `false` as "retry next heartbeat" is safe. - **Findings:** **MREL-1 (LIVE, partially superseded)** — ARC-H6 already GCs committed journals past 24 h and caps dead letters at 100, but the GC filter's `else true` keeps EVERY non-committed entry: a prepared journal (full report body) is never deleted — not on dead-lettering (exhausted 8 attempts / expired 24 h / quarantined), not when its dead letter drops off the 100-cap, not as a legacy orphan. Idle queues never GC either (early return at :864 fires before the GC block). **MBR-1 (LIVE)** — `onMeshMessage('null')` throws: `JSON.parse('null')` succeeds, then `null.peerId` throws before `??` applies. No test in the repo exercises the handler at all. **MBR-2 (LIVE)** — `pruneReplayCache` evicts the oldest past 4096, contradicting the file's own header comment ("never 'when the set got big'"); the existing "bounds the cache" test asserts the buggy behavior.
- **Implemented:** **MREL-1 (D1+D2)** — new `collectJournalGarbage(journal, pendingIds, now)` helper: committed past 24 h dropped (ARC-H6 rule, unchanged), prepared dropped unless its item is still pending, delivered kept; the flush GC block uses it with `nextPending` ids; the idle path (pending empty) runs the same GC and writes only when something was removed (re-reads inside `serializeQueueMutation`, bails if a concurrent enqueue raced in). **MBR-1 (D3a)** — object guard after parse: `if (typeof parsed !== "object" || parsed === null || Array.isArray(parsed)) return;` (drops null + bare strings/numbers/booleans/arrays, matching the handler's "don't forward raw text" comment). **MBR-2 (D4a)** — the capacity-eviction `while` loop deleted from `pruneReplayCache` (age-only purge now); `MAX_REPLAY_ENTRIES` constant removed (verified only 2 references); header comment's "5 minutes" corrected to the real 12-minute retention (10-min TTL + 2-min skew); the test file's Round-8 header comment corrected the same way. Do-not-touch respected: native `MeshInbound.kt` (D5, follow-up), `useMeshSync.ts`, `RELAY_MAX_DEAD_LETTERS` cap, `commitDeliveredJournal`/`rebuildPendingFromJournal`/`mergeReplicaQueueStates`/`makeCapacityTransition` semantics, `?? "unknown"` peerId fallback, TTL/skew constants, ARC-H6 committed rule, `checkAndRecordRelayHash` (fail-closed, not the defect).

(2) Native "5-minute TTL" comment (`MeshInbound.kt:37`) is likewise stale — correct in the same later part as (1) (native freshness-window value NOT verified). (3) MBR-1 assumption (D3): if a legitimate native payload is ever a bare JSON string/number/boolean, the object guard drops it — narrow to null-only if that ever proves real. (4) MREL-1 adjacent: `volatilePending` items (persistence-failed path) have no journal at all — a later part may consider whether volatile items need any recovery record. (5) Residual NOT in the prep (parent): the `!changed` early return in `flushQueueInternal` also skips the GC when pending is non-empty but nothing changed (all items backing off) — the committed age-GC then waits for a flush that changes something; a later part may run the GC there too. (6) Standing follow-ups (Fixes 29/27/25c/38/39/40/41/42/43/44/45 items) — unchanged by this part.
- **Fix 46 CodeRabbit retained concerns (summary only, NOT actionable — no comment IDs, no requested changes; parent judged both non-blocking, recorded here):** (a) Medium · security · inferred — removing the 4096-entry eviction makes replay-cache memory depend on admitted-message volume in the 12-min window; a token-authenticated peer could grow memory on connected pages. This is the D4 trade-off already analyzed in the prep (entries recorded only after validation/authentication; per-page-lifetime map) — accepted by design. (b) Medium · reliability · inferred — the idle cleanup's full-snapshot write does not compare the stored revision, so it could overwrite a report durably enqueued by another same-origin page. The idle write re-reads the latest state inside `serializeQueueMutation` and bails when pending is non-empty, but the cross-tab read→write window has no lock — the SAME shape as the pre-existing flush-path reconciliation write, so this is a pre-existing cross-tab race pattern, not introduced by Fix 46; a later part may add a revision check to both write paths.

All 7 decisions D1–D7 resolved to the prep's recommendations.
- **Parent spot-verification at cb5ef78 (all citations hold):** firms-proxy.js header example :9 (`-17,18,25,38`, spans 42° > cap), validation :94–106, target :117–118 (raw `bbox`) ✓; deploy-rules.mjs header :2–4 (no SA_KEY_FILE), usage :8, loadDotEnv :16–30 (regex :19, skip :22, guard :27), loadServiceAccountFile :32–34, main :42–58 (`readFileSync(null)` :58), tail `main().catch(...)` ✓; screenshot.mjs 86 lines, spawn :19–31 (top-level), waitForServer :36–49 (hard-coded 3000 :40), top-level Playwright import :51, try :52, browser :56 block-scoped, close :77 success-only, finally :85–86 (server.kill only) ✓; server/config.ts parsePort :58–61 (`Number()` + integer + 1..65535, else fallback) ✓; `google-auth-library` absent from package.json, in pnpm-lock.yaml :7317/:7366 as transitive via firebase-tools 10.9.1, firebase-tools ^15.28.2 at package.json :72 ✓. **Guard-shape verification (parent, before implementing):** Node resolves `process.argv[1]` to an ABSOLUTE path even when invoked relatively (probed in /tmp), so the `fileURLToPath(import.meta.url) === process.argv[1]` guard works for `node scripts/deploy-rules.mjs` from the repo root — proven live: the run hit the new D2 fail-fast ("✖ No Firebase service account configured…").
- **Findings:** **FPROXY-1 (LIVE)** — `,20,10,30` → 200, upstream called with the malformed path verbatim (`Number("")` is 0, so the finite check at :95 passes; target at :117–118 uses the RAW bbox string). Also `5,,20,30` → 200; `20.0,10,30,40` → 200 forwarded verbatim. **DRULES-1 (LIVE)** — `SA_KEY_FILE` only → `loadServiceAccountFile` returns `null`; the documented invocation is ignored, and the no-credential path dies at `readFileSync(null)` (:58). **DRULES-2 (LIVE)** — unquoted and double-quoted single-line JSON both discarded (`{}`); single-quoted works. Load-bearing trap: removing the :22 skip naively lets the general parser overwrite a multi-line single-quoted value with its truncated first line (:27 checks `process.env`, not `out`). **SHOT-1 (LIVE)** — `browser` block-scoped in try (:56), close only on success (:77), finally kills only the server (:86). **SHOT-2 (LIVE)** — child inherits `PORT` (binds `parsePort(process.env.PORT, 3000)`), script hard-codes 3000 at :40/:59/:73, and never checks the child is alive — two distinct failure modes (wrong port; stranger on 3000 after `EADDRINUSE` death).
- **Implemented:** **FPROXY-1 (D1a)** — reject any coordinate segment empty after `trim()`; build the upstream target from the validated numeric tuple `[minLng, minLat, maxLng, maxLat].join(",")` (normalizes `20.0`/`0x10`/`1e1` to canonical decimals); header example corrected `-17,18,25,38` → `-17,18,20,38` (spans 37°, returns 200). **DRULES-1 (D2a)** — `SA_KEY_FILE` added to the `loadServiceAccountFile` chain AFTER the existing names (both `process.env` and the `.env`-loaded `env` object); header comment lists it; fail-fast `if (!credentials && !keyFile) throw new Error("No Firebase service account configured — set FIREBASE_SERVICE_ACCOUNT, SA_KEY_FILE, SA_FILE, or FIREBASE_SERVICE_ACCOUNT_PATH")` placed BEFORE `new GoogleAuth`. **DRULES-2 (D3a)** — regex extended to both quote styles + the :22 skip removed + the :27 guard strengthened to `!(m[1] in process.env) && !(m[1] in out)` (the `in out` guard is load-bearing against the overwrite trap). Unquoted multi-line JSON stays unsupported (documented in the header comment — a parser limitation, not a defect). **SHOT-1 (D4a)** — `let browser;` before the try, `if (browser) await browser.close().catch(() => {})` in the `finally` BEFORE `server.kill()` (the explicit success-path close was REMOVED — the finally closes exactly once on both paths; a double close would break the test's `toHaveBeenCalledTimes(1)`). **SHOT-2 (D6a)** — `resolvePort(env)` mirroring `parsePort` exactly (not `Number(...) || 3000` — `PORT="4001.5"` diverges) used in `waitForServer` and both `goto`s; child-alive check (`exitCode`/`signalCode` non-null) at the top of the wait loop AND after a successful readiness probe, throwing `Screenshot server exited during startup (exit code …)` — the re-throw guard in the catch (`err.message.startsWith("Screenshot server exited")`) keeps the throw from being swallowed as "not up yet". **D5** — `captureScreenshots(deps = {})` extracted with injectable `{ chromium, spawnFn, fetchFn, env, outDir }` (defaults: lazy `@playwright/test` import, `node:child_process` spawn, global fetch, `process.env`, `docs/screenshots`); the spawn moved INSIDE the function (required for `spawnFn` substitution); the dist check stays at entry level; main-guard (`fileURLToPath(import.meta.url) === process.argv[1]`) in both scripts — first main-guards in the repo. `deploy-rules.mjs` exports `loadDotEnv`, `loadServiceAccountFile`, `loadServiceAccountJson`; `screenshot.mjs` exports `resolvePort`, `captureScreenshots`. Do-not-touch respected: the M5 auth gate, `ALLOWED_SOURCES`, `MAX_DAYS`, cache TTL, 40° cap, `/new-key` flow, the release PATCH-then-POST flow, `GoogleAuth` scopes, `parsePort`, `satellite.ts`, `playwright.config.ts`, `src/` and `server/` anything. **Parent self-caught slip (fixed in-flight):** the rewrite initially changed the spawn env's `JWT_SECRET` from `"screenshot-secret"` to `"screenshot-local-only"` — an unplanned value change; reverted to the original.
- **D7 scope expansion:** `google-auth-library` declared as a direct dependency (`"google-auth-library": "10.9.1"` in package.json, alphabetical position after `firebase-admin`) — it resolved only transitively via firebase-tools before. Lockfile regenerated in /tmp with `pnpm-workspace.yaml` copied (the Fix 45 recipe — clone `pnpm install` fails with `EPERM fchown`); diff verified: the new direct dep + a transitive `google-logging-utils` 1.1.3 → 1.2.0 bump in 2 places (the resolver's re-resolution of google-auth-library 10.9.1's own deps — accepted, recorded here); written back with `cat` (inode preserved).
- **Tests (+17 new, 0 updated assertions):** FPROXY +4 in `tests/firms-proxy.test.ts` (mocked global fetch; never use the header's example bbox as a valid control — it 400s); DRULES +8 in `tests/deploy-rules.test.ts` (temp dir per test + `process.chdir`, scrubbed env keys saved/restored; subprocess fail-fast test with `{ timeout: 15_000 }` — lesson (qq)); SHOT +5 in `tests/screenshot.test.ts` (fake chromium/spawn/fetch; fake child stdout/stderr are `EventEmitter`s for the `.on("data")` listeners; desktop vs mobile page distinguished by `opts?.isMobile`). **Prep correction (parent, DRULES-2 regex): the prep's prescribed regex `/^FIREBASE_SERVICE_ACCOUNT=(?:'([\s\S]*?)'|"([\s\S]*?)")$/m` is WRONG for pretty-printed double-quoted JSON — the lazy match stops at the FIRST `"` at a line end, which is the closing quote of the last property's value (`"a@b.c"`), truncating the value before its closing `}` (proven: captured value ends at `"a@b.c"`, `JSON.parse` throws "Unterminated string in JSON at position 63"). The implementation adds a lookahead so the value ends only at a quote followed by a new KEY= assignment or end of input: `(?=\n[A-Z0-9_]+=|\n*(?![\s\S]))` (`(?![\s\S])` is the flag-independent end-of-input anchor — `$` with the `m` flag would match at any line end). Single quotes need no such guard: valid JSON never has `'` as a line's last character.** **Harness fix (parent-caught): under the jsdom-based React config, `import.meta.url` is NOT a `file:` URL, so both `.mjs`-importing test files fail at import with `TypeError: The URL must be of scheme file` — added `// @vitest-environment node` as line 1 of `tests/deploy-rules.test.ts` and `tests/screenshot.test.ts` (the symmetric counterpart of the repo's `// @vitest-environment jsdom` pattern); both files are Node-only anyway (fs, child_process, spawn).**
- **Fail-without-fix (proven):** stashing the 3 source files (`worker/firms-proxy.js`, `scripts/deploy-rules.mjs`, `scripts/screenshot.mjs`) fails exactly **16** tests — the 12 finding tests + 4 control tests as import collateral (lesson (jj)): the DRULES file's named imports are `undefined` on old code (6 finding + 2 controls), the SHOT file's `captureScreenshots` import is `undefined` on old code (3 finding + 2 controls); only FPROXY discriminates cleanly (3 finding fail, 1 control passes). On old code the DRULES subprocess test fails by message mismatch in ~3.4 s (not by the 10 s exec timeout) — noted as the prep predicted. Targeted 3 files on new code: 17/17 under BOTH configs.
- **Fix 47 gates:** lint 0, build pass, server **1018/1018** (93 files; 1001 + 17). React **1398/1398** full run (128 files; 1381 + 17) — CLEAN, zero failures (not even the usual safe-evacuation flake). test:e2e not runnable in this VM (Firebase emulator needs Java) — CI covers it.
- **Fix 47 follow-ups (prep §5, recorded):** (1) Unquoted multi-line `FIREBASE_SERVICE_ACCOUNT` in `.env` cannot be supported by the line-based parser (no terminator) — documented in the script header; multi-line values need single or double quotes. (2) SHOT-2 identity check (stronger guarantee) would need `/api/health` to echo a run-marker env var — a `server/` change, for a later part; the D6 exit-code check closes both failure modes without it. (3) Worker deployment config is not versioned (`worker/` has only `firms-proxy.js` — no `wrangler.toml` or deploy script in the repo) — a separate ops question, not a finding. - **Comment 4685534436 — `scripts/deploy-rules.mjs:32` · Minor · Functional Correctness ("Allow blank and comment lines after quoted credentials"):** the Fix 47 lookahead required the very next line after the closing quote to be a `KEY=` assignment or end of input — a blank line (or CRLF endings) after the value made the whole SA regex fail, the line parser stored only `'{'`, and `loadServiceAccountJson` threw. **Proven live by the parent before fixing** (node probe on the 47 code: blank-line case AND CRLF case both return `'{'` and throw `Unexpected token`). **Fix:** the lookahead now tolerates blank lines, full-line comment lines, trailing spaces, and CRLF after the closing quote (`(?=(?:[ \t]*\r?\n(?:[ \t]*\r?\n|[ \t]*#[^\n]*\r?\n)*[ \t]*(?:[A-Z0-9_]+=|(?![\s\S]))|[ \t]*(?![\s\S])))`), while the inner-property-quote protection still holds (an inner `"` at a line end is followed by more JSON, not a KEY=/EOF — traced by hand). **Tests +3** in `tests/deploy-rules.test.ts`: single-quoted multi-line + blank line + KEY after; double-quoted multi-line + comment line + KEY after; double-quoted multi-line CRLF + blank line + KEY after.
- **Comment 4685534445 — `scripts/screenshot.mjs:84` · Minor · Stability ("Include dependency loading in the server cleanup scope"):** the `await import("@playwright/test")` sat between the spawn (:40) and the try (:86) — a failed import left the spawned child running with nothing to kill it. **Fix:** resolve `chromiumImpl` BEFORE spawning the server; if the import rejects, nothing has started yet. **Test +1** in `tests/screenshot.test.ts`: `vi.mock("@playwright/test")` with a `chromium` getter that throws — the dynamic import resolves but resolving the dependency fails at the import line, before the spawn; asserts rejection with "playwright load boom" AND `spawnFn` never called. **Harness note (parent):** a mock factory that throws directly does NOT work — vitest wraps it in "[vitest] There was an error when mocking a module…"; the throwing-getter shape is the working one (verified 6/6). The other tests pass a chromium dep, so they never touch the mock.
- **Comment 4685534460 — `tests/deploy-rules.test.ts:39` · Minor · Functional Correctness ("Remove stubs before restoring the saved environment"):** `vi.unstubAllEnvs()` ran AFTER the `savedEnv` restore loop — if a key pre-existed, the restore loop's value was then removed again by the unstub. **Fix:** `vi.unstubAllEnvs()` moved before the restore loop. No test for this one (test-harness ordering fix; practical impact nil in this suite — keys are re-deleted per test and files run in separate workers — verified by reading).
- **CodeRabbit summary-only retained concerns (no comment IDs, not blocking):** Medium · security · inferred — `SA_KEY_FILE` in process env now overrides legacy `.env` key paths (the finding's intended fix itself) and duplicate `.env` assignments now select the first value rather than the last (the deliberate first-wins guard for the multi-line fix); Low · security · inferred — after a capture error, the code awaits browser shutdown before requesting server termination, so if `browser.close()` never settles the server can remain listening (`.catch` handles rejection, not a hang — recorded as a follow-up below, not fixed in 47b).
- **Tests (+4 new, 0 updated assertions):** targeted 3 files on new code: 21/21 under BOTH configs.
- **Fail-without-fix (proven):** stashing the 2 behavior files (`scripts/deploy-rules.mjs`, `scripts/screenshot.mjs`) fails exactly **4** tests — the 3 new DRULES tests + the new SHOT test; all 13 others pass on old code.

## Fix 47c — CodeQL HIGH + CodeRabbit round on PR #178 (1 line, 1 test)

- **CI watch verdict at a9a6725: DO NOT MERGE** — all of the PR's own functional checks green (lint-and-test, build-and-test, e2e, both Analyzes, release, Vercel; instrumented in progress, the known non-blocker), old 3 CodeRabbit comments verified fixed in code at a9a6725, but the 47b round introduced **one underlying defect with two reports, both on `scripts/deploy-rules.mjs:37`**: **CodeQL HIGH alert #21** ("Inefficient regular expression — this part of the regular expression may cause exponential backtracking on strings containing many repetitions of '\r\n#'") and **CodeRabbit Minor 4685825264** ("Exclude carriage returns from comment-line matches in the `.env` separator" — 20 CRLF comment lines ~0.5 s, 24 lines > 3 s). **Root cause:** the 47b comment alternative was `[ \t]*#[^\n]*\r?\n` — `[^\n]` also matches `\r`, so on CRLF comment lines the `\r` had two match paths and the combinations multiplied across lines whenever the lookahead failed. **Verified live by the parent before fixing:** 24 CRLF comment lines followed by a non-KEY line took **6,606 ms** on the 47b code (and the value fell back to `'{'`); the same shape with 30 lines took **83 s** in the fail-without-fix proof.
- **Fix:** one character-class change — `[^\n]*` → `[^\r\n]*` in the comment alternative, so each comment line has exactly one match path. **Re-probed after the fix:** fail-shape 6,606 ms → **1 ms**; success-shape 0 ms with correct parse.
- **Test +1** in `tests/deploy-rules.test.ts`: 30 CRLF comment lines before a non-KEY line — asserts `loadDotEnv()` returns in under 2 s (generous on this VM; on old code it would take minutes and the test timeout fires first) and that `SA_KEY_FILE` is still read.

No overlap with Fix 49 files except `Models.kt` (different functions).
- **Prep corrections (parent-caught, substance unchanged):** the prep predicted "zero line shift" from Fix 49 — WRONG: Fix 49's comment insertions shifted `Models.kt` by **+7** (`parseOsrmAlternatives` signature at :245 at 1fc3ebc, not :238) and `MapFragment.kt` by **+17** (caller at :754, not :737). `OfflineQueue.kt` has zero drift (still 235 lines; all anchors match the prep exactly).
- **Findings:** **NMODEL-2 (LIVE)** — `parseOsrmAlternatives` (`Models.kt:245–263`) wraps the ENTIRE three-route loop in a single `runCatching`; one malformed alternative (missing geometry, short coordinate pair, missing distance/duration) discards all routes parsed so far and returns `null`, contradicting its own kdoc ("never the empty list when route[0] parsed") and the file's per-item isolation rule (:12). Only caller `MapFragment.kt:754` takes the honest fallback on `null` (straight-line distance, no invented route). **NOFF-1 (LIVE)** — `size()` (:103) and `restoreAll` (:150–157) count waiting PLUS in-flight, but `evictForAdmission`'s gate (:227) checks waiting only. Verified arithmetic: 60 waiting → `reserveDue(3)` → 57 waiting/3 in-flight → 3 enqueues with no eviction → 60/3 → failed commit reinserts all three → 63 waiting. **The overshoot is bounded at capacity + batch size (63) in the single-drain flow, not unbounded** — the defect is the violated advertised bound, not a leak.
- **Decisions (all prep recommendations, all implemented):** D1: NMODEL-2 — move the `runCatching` inside the loop (per-item isolation, the bare-`runCatching` shape `parseReports` already uses); `null` only when zero routes parse; top-level missing-`routes` still returns `null`; the `pts.isNotEmpty()` gate and the 3-route cap unchanged; zero caller change. D2: NOFF-1 — gate becomes `items.size + inFlight.size < capacity` + the load-bearing empty-deque guard (`if (items.isEmpty()) return` — without it, an all-in-flight queue would compute victim index `0` on an empty deque and throw `IndexOutOfBoundsException` on `removeAt(0)`); victim selection untouched (oldest already-attempted, never-attempted shielded, in-flight never candidates — only counted). **Parent deviation (doc-only, recorded):** the prep asked to update only the `evictForAdmission` kdoc; the implementation ALSO rewords the `reserveDue` docstring ("they are invisible to enqueue-eviction" → "they are never eviction candidates") — the old wording became false once in-flight entries count toward the admission total.
- **Tests (+5 new, 0 updated assertions):** NMODEL-2 +0 JVM by design (org.json unavailable on the JVM test host — `Models.kt:9–11`; NSVC-5 precedent). ## Fix 51 — Native camera and photo handling (NCAM, NPHOTO, NREPORT, all Minor)

- **Branch:** `fix/wildfire-51-camera-photo` from main **8c62b09** (explicit SHA — the clone's local `main` is STALE at fe74280; stash pattern used; the Fix 50 merge record rides uncommitted in FIX_PLAN.md). **Prep:** `~/workspace/wildfire-fix-prep/fix51-prep.md` (130 lines, base 1fc3ebc) — **parent read it in full and re-verified ALL anchors at 8c62b09 — zero drift from Fix 50** (Fix 50 touches `Models.kt`/`OfflineQueue.kt` only). Triage row 82: "Native camera and photo handling (NCAM, NPHOTO, NREPORT)" — **SCOPE CORRECTION: NPHOTO-2 is already fixed by Fix 19b** (`PhotoPipeline.kt:57` reads `} while (bytes.size > maxBytes && quality >= FLOOR_QUALITY)` — the `>=` is there, matching the Fix 19b record at FIX_PLAN.md:1378–1380, one character `>` → `>=`, PR #150 follow-up). **Fix 51 closes 3 live findings, not 4** — reconcile −1 for NPHOTO-2, same pattern as NREP-6 in Fix 49. Touches `CameraCaptureFragment.kt` + `PhotoPipeline.kt` + `ReportFragment.kt`; no `server/`, no TS, no workflow changes. No overlap with Fix 50 files; no overlap with Fix 49 files.
- **Findings:** **NCAM-4 (LIVE)** — `stampExecutor` (`CameraCaptureFragment.kt:109`, lazy `Executors.newSingleThreadExecutor()`, only use `:450`) has no `shutdown()` anywhere in the file and no `onDestroy` override; every camera visit that takes a photo leaks one non-daemon worker thread for the process lifetime. **NPHOTO-3 (LIVE)** — `compressWithinBudget` (`PhotoPipeline.kt:52–61`) discards `Bitmap.compress`'s boolean; a `false` encode leaves an empty stream, `0 > maxBytes` is false, and the 0-byte array is returned as success. Only caller `ReportFragment.kt:414` checks only `bytes == null` and announces the empty image as attached (0 KB). **NREPORT-2 (LIVE)** — the guard at `ReportFragment.kt:287` reads `if (!geoFailed && now < geoBackoffUntilMs) return`; a failure sets `geoFailed=true` + 60 s backoff, so the guard can never fire again — every 5 s location publish re-fires a lookup during the outage (up to ~12 lookups per 60 s outage). **Second-order effect (prep probe):** the success path (`:314`) sets `geoFailed = false` but never resets `geoBackoffUntilMs`, so a failure→success sequence with partial autofill silently blocks the wilaya lookup for up to 60 s.
- **Decisions (all prep recommendations, all implemented):** D1: NCAM-4 — `stampExecutor.shutdown()` in `onDestroyView` (the file's own F2 convention), `shutdown()` not `shutdownNow()` so an in-flight stamp finishes (delivery rides `lifecycleScope`, NCAM-1); re-use hazard dismissed (no `setRetainInstance`, no detach/attach, instance destroyed on pop/config change; `onImageSaved` is `isAdded`-gated and `imageCapture` is nulled in `onDestroyView`, so no new `execute()` can be submitted after shutdown); the lazy-init quirk is stated in the comment (accessing the val merely allocates — `ThreadPoolExecutor` spawns its worker only on first task submission, so no thread is created for a photo-less visit). D2: NPHOTO-3 — fail fast: capture the boolean, `if (!ok) return null` inside the loop; treating `false` as oversized-and-continue rejected (encoder failures are deterministic across qualities); a call-site `isEmpty` check rejected (single fix site). D3: NREPORT-2 — guard becomes `if (now < geoBackoffUntilMs) return` (drop the `!geoFailed &&` conjunction) + success branch adds `geoBackoffUntilMs = 0L`; `geoFailed` stays (explicit-retry path + status); pure-`GeoBackoff` extraction rejected (single comparison, directly readable).
- **Tests: +0 new, 0 updated.** All three live findings are NSVC-5 read-through + CI build — no JVM seam exists for any of them (no fragment harness / no Robolectric; `Bitmap` stubbed on the JVM classpath; fragment method). NPHOTO-2 +0 by construction (already fixed; closure verified by reading `:52–61` only).

(1) **Monotonic clock (Minor · Stability & Availability):** the Fix 51 deadline rode `System.currentTimeMillis()` at both the guard (:291) and the set (:308) — a backward device-clock jump after a failure would stretch the 60 s suppression window; manual retry still worked (:237 reset). Fix: `SystemClock.elapsedRealtime()` in both places (+ import), and the field renamed `geoBackoffUntilMs` → `geoBackoffUntilElapsedMs` (private, 6 mechanical sites — decl, :237, :291, :308, :324, :489 — so a future reader cannot mistake it for a wall-clock value; repo-wide grep confirms no other file references it). (2) **Offline wilaya recompute during backoff (Minor · Data Integrity & Integration):** the failure branch fills the wilaya field from `Wilayas.nearest(lat, lng)` but never sets `autofilledWilaya = true` (only set on the success path :332 and manual edit :137), so the caller keeps invoking `reverseGeocode` on every publish and the :291 guard returns before the fallback can recompute — a boundary crossing inside the 60 s window leaves the previous fix's wilaya in the field while `submit()` (:451) sends the NEW fix's coordinates with it (verified: submit reads `wilayaInput?.text`). Fix: the guard branch now recomputes `Wilayas.nearest(lat, lng)` and refreshes the field when `!autofilledWilaya` — no network request is started there; network results and user edits are never overwritten. **49b lesson applied:** grep over all added Kotlin lines — zero `/*` occurrences; all new comments are `//` line comments. ## Fix 55 — Native mesh protocol and cryptography (NCRYPTO, NIN, NSVC)
- **Prep:** `~/workspace/wildfire-fix-prep/fix55-prep.md` (159 lines, base e30856b) — parent read it in full and re-verified ALL anchors at c471c20 — zero drift (CryptoEngine :174–180/:231–236/:284–298, MeshInbound :37/:44–48/:50/:131/:173–176/:197–201, MeshWire wirePrefix :437 + seenMessageHash :228, MeshService :43 companion/:73/:76/:78/:88/:273/:327/:350/:398–414/:598/:772–773/:830/:842–843/:900/:929/:959/:966/:1055/:1091/:1097; MESSAGE_TTL_MS = 10 min at MeshQueue.kt:61, FORWARDED_MARKER_TTL_MS = 300_000L at :65). Triage row 86: "Native mesh protocol and cryptography (NCRYPTO, NIN, NSVC)" — **SCOPE CORRECTION: NSVC-2 is already fixed** (Fix 7b, commit d1e2105 — `sightAndCheckAdmission` gates at :327/:350/:598, verified at c471c20) — do NOT re-fix. **4 live findings** (NCRYPTO-1, NCRYPTO-2, NIN-1, NSVC-3) + NSVC-4 live with a design tension (doc-only) + Fix 46 follow-up (2) stale TTL comment.
- **Branch:** `fix/wildfire-55-mesh-crypto` from **c471c20** (stash pattern; `git log --oneline -1` verified = c471c20; stash pop clean; the Fix 54 merge record rode uncommitted in FIX_PLAN.md into this branch).
- **Findings (verified at c471c20):** **NCRYPTO-1** (Minor) — decode catch sets `legacyCorrupt = true` (:236), promising the undecodable material is "KEPT (forensics, never overwritten)" (:231–234), but the software fallback writes a fresh pair to the SAME prefs keys (:295–296), destroying it; the "only a missing-clean install reaches this point" comment (:284–285) is FALSE on the corrupt-legacy path. Impact bounded today: the identity key signs nothing on the wire (:174–180). **NCRYPTO-2** (Minor) — fallback ignores `commit()`'s boolean (:297) and records `SOFTWARE_FALLBACK` (:298) as a stable init; a failed write leaves a memory-only identity, contradicting the process-death continuity rationale (:286–289). **NIN-1** (Minor) — PoW is verified against `wirePrefix(messageId, origEphemeralId)` only (:131) while replay admission keys on `(messageId, nonce)` (:173–176); one solved PoW admits arbitrarily many signed nonce variants (work-amortization gap, not signature forgery — the nonce IS in the signed metadata). **NSVC-3** (Minor) — empty batches double `trickleInterval` to 30 s (:966) but `scheduleNextTrickle` uses only `if (isSleeping) SLEEP_INTERVAL else TRICKLE_I_MIN` (:900), ignoring it; awake ticks stay at 1 Hz while the counter claims back-off. **NSVC-4** (Minor, design tension) — reputation is keyed by the ADVERTISED (ephemeral) key (:398–404), rotation is hourly (:76, :273, :929) so every rotation resets the score to 50; the :398–399 comment "reputation is anchored on the KEY (stable identity)" is FALSE across rotations. Re-keying to the identity key would put the identity key on the wire, contradicting the deliberate audit-round-11 privacy design (identity signs NOTHING, CryptoEngine :174–180) — owner decision for a later part, NOT re-keyed here.
- **Fixes IMPLEMENTED (all prep recommendations):** D1 NCRYPTO-1: in the fallback, if `legacyCorrupt`, copy both legacy values to forensic keys (`IDENTITY_PUB_KEY + "_corrupt"`, `IDENTITY_PRIV_KEY + "_corrupt"`) in the SAME edit chain BEFORE writing the fresh pair; the :284–285 comment corrected; forensic keys are write-only retention (nothing ever reads them — stated in the code comment). `recordIdentityStorage(..., regenerated = legacyCorrupt)` stays. D2 NCRYPTO-2: `val committed = edit...commit()` then `check(committed) { "identity storage commit failed — refusing a memory-only identity" }` — fail closed, same `check()` style as the policy gate at :290. **Parent confirms the fail-closed check** (prep asked for confirmation; the softer MEMORY_ONLY alternative is NOT used). D3 NIN-1: `wirePrefix(messageId, ephemeralId, nonce)` — nonce appended length-prefixed, same style as `seenMessageHash` :228; all 3 call sites change together (verifier MeshInbound :131; relay solver MeshService :773; broadcast solver :842). Broadcast already picks `nonce` (:830) BEFORE solving — passed directly. Relay REORDERED: nonce picked first, then solved with it in the prefix, then admitted with that nonce + powNonce. `wirePrefixIsCanonicalAcrossFieldBoundaries` gains the nonce component. The seen-cache key is untouched (Fix 16c remainder is a separate content-dedup question; a relay legitimately mints a fresh nonce + fresh PoW per copy). D4 NSVC-3: pure `nextTrickleDelayMs(isSleeping, trickleInterval)` in the companion object, used by `scheduleNextTrickle`; `managePower` already sets the interval on sleep/wake (:1091/:1097). D5 NSVC-4: comment corrected to state the truth (reputation is per-ephemeral-key; a rotation resets the score); no re-keying. Fix 46 follow-up (2): MeshInbound.kt :37 "5-minute TTL" → 10-min TTL (MESSAGE_TTL_MS = 10 min, + 2-min skew = 12-min retention); :44–48 budget math ~13.6/s → ~6.8/s over 10 min (4096/600). Fix 46 follow-up (1) (seen-cache cap) STAYS OUT — documented trade-off, no evidence-based target, Fix 61 / owner decision; NIN-1 does not interact with it.
- **Parent decisions recorded:** (1) D2 fail-closed `check()` confirmed (see above). (2) D4 seam location: companion-object function (SLEEP_INTERVAL + TRICKLE_I_MIN are companion consts; the companion's only non-const member is `SecureRandom()`, pure JVM — so a JVM test calling it should load; if CI `build-and-test` shows a load failure, move it to a standalone pure object per the Fix 49 SendPolicy precedent). (3) D3 wire-contract break noted: old frames (solved without nonce) FAIL verification after this lands — acceptable (mesh is ephemeral, no persisted PoW; no migration) — stated in the PR body.

- **Fix 55c (CodeRabbit round on PR #186, watch 2 DO NOT MERGE at d601dc7):** all own checks green (lint-and-test, build-and-test — tenth proof new Kotlin tests run green in CI, release, e2e, both Analyze; CodeQL neutral accepted; instrumented in progress = known non-blocker; no Vercel check-run on this head) + CodeRabbit check run success, held by 3 NEW findings on the f9f5851 → d601dc7 delta (review id 6050770065), all verified REAL by the parent in code at d601dc7: (1) Major 4689465986 (MeshService.kt:1043) — per-peer copies share msg.nonce, so an undecryptable relay copy admitted to the seen-cache (admission is after PoW/signature but before decrypt) causes the intended copy to be rejected as REPLAY; fix = the seen-cache key now covers the payload too (`seenMessageHash(messageId, nonce, payloadB64)` — per-peer copies carry distinct ciphertexts, an identical frame still hashes identically; alternatives rejected: admit-after-decrypt (weakens replay protection for undecryptable frames + changes the audit-pinned gate order) and per-peer PoW re-solve (a solve per peer per message, queue-model change). (2) Major (CryptoEngine.kt:311, outside-diff) — the new `check(committed)` throws in `loadOrCreateIdentityKeyPair` and `onCreate → initCrypto → initialize` caught nothing, so a storage failure killed the service startup and could terminate the process; fix = `initCrypto()` returns Boolean, `onCreate` logs + `stopSelf()` + returns on failure (fail-closed preserved: no mesh without an identity; `onDestroy` already does the full teardown incl. `stopForeground`). (3) Minor (CryptoEngine.kt:296, outside-diff) — an incomplete legacy pair (only one half present) left `legacyCorrupt` false, so the fallback overwrote that half without a forensic copy; fix = an incomplete pair now sets `legacyCorrupt = true` and the forensic copy null-guards each half. **+1 test, 2 updated** (MeshInboundTest +1 `sameNonceDifferentPayloadHashesApart`; MeshWireTest 2 updated to 3-arg `seenMessageHash` + a payload-difference case). Judged a KNOWN FLAKE (not a PR regression): this PR touches zero TS/TSX; the test passed locally at this exact content (full run 1411/1412, the only local failure was the known report-form-hooks flake, 41/41 isolated) and 11/11 in isolation (`~/workspace/react-55c-safe-evac-isolated.log`); the test's own Fix 26b comment documents the Leaflet-init slowness under parallel load, and the flake migrates between tests run to run. CodeRabbit review id 6051093099 posted 3 NEW findings, all verified REAL by the parent in code at a68ae00: (1) Major 4689732564 (MeshInbound.kt:178, CWE-799, "Heavy lift") — one PoW solve per (messageId, ephemeralId, nonce) can sign arbitrarily many payload variants, and the 55c payload-keyed admission admits every variant (PoW difficulty is 8 bits ≈ 256 hashes, and evaluate has no per-sender rate limit, so the amplification is real though the PoW was never a strong bound); fix = per-triple variant budget — `seenTripleHash(messageId, nonce)` + `seenTripleCounts` (TripleAdmission{count, firstSeenMs}) + `MAX_VARIANTS_PER_TRIPLE = 16` (generous headroom over any plausible peer count; bounds amplification to 16x), enforced after signature before payload-key admission, rejected as REPLAY (silent, no reputation hit), with rollback on true replays so replays do not consume the budget, and GC in `sweepReplayWindow` on the same cutoff. Alternative rejected: binding PoW to the payload (per-peer re-solve, queue-model change). (2) Minor, same comment (MeshInbound.kt:178) — an undecryptable copy can DELAY the intended copy at a shared next hop: send state is keyed by messageId alone (`forwardedMessages["$endpointId:$messageId"]`, `deliveredTargets[messageId]`, `forwardsPerMessage[messageId]` vs TRICKLE_K=3), so the second copy's delivery to a peer waits out the forwarded-marker TTL (~5 min) — delayed, not dropped; direct path unaffected. NOT fixed in this part — requires re-keying MeshQueue + MeshService send state by (messageId, payload), a redesign for a later part; recorded as a Fix 55 follow-up (next bullet). (3) Minor 4689732575 (MeshService.kt:173) — fail-closed across same-process restarts: Android updates the in-memory preference map before commit() reports a disk-write failure, so a same-process retry would read the unpersisted replacement pair through the legacy branch and accept the memory-only identity; fix = process-local `identityCommitFailed` flag in CryptoEngine — set when the fallback commit fails, the legacy branch is skipped while set (keystore/fallback path mints a fresh pair and requires a successful commit), cleared when a commit succeeds; a fresh process reads from disk where the failed pair was never persisted. **+2 tests, +2 updated** (MeshInboundTest +2 `variantBudgetRejectsBeyondMaxVariantsPerTriple` + `sweepReplayWindowExpiresTripleCounts`; MeshWireTest +2 `seenTripleHash` canonical/ambiguity). Finding (3) +0 JVM (CryptoEngine has no JVM seam; proof is review + CI build). ## Fix 56 — Native wire size accounting (NWIRE-1)

- **Base:** f1f6557 (Fix 55 merged as PR #186, 2026-11-25 ~05:32 CET). Branch `fix/wildfire-56-mesh-plaintext-limit`. Triage row 87: NWIRE-1 (Minor, `MeshWire.kt` per triage — the fix lands in `MeshService.kt` + tests in `MeshWireTest.kt`; `MeshWire.kt` itself untouched, its `require` is a correct guard). **NO scope correction: NWIRE-1 LIVE** (re-verified at f1f6557: `MAX_PLAINTEXT_BYTES = 256 * 1024` at MeshService.kt:101; compress `require` at MeshWire.kt:110; broadcast gate :859, relay gate :702, inbound check :711; `sendToTarget` :1227, frameToJson catch :1270–1278, compress call :1279, per-peer catch + quarantine :1081–1082).
- **Finding:** `broadcastMessage` accepts plaintext up to 262144 bytes, but AES-GCM (+16-byte tag) + base64 (×4/3) + frame metadata must fit the SAME 262144 ceiling in `MeshWire.compress`. 196608 plaintext → 196624 ciphertext → 262168 base64 chars — over the ceiling before any metadata. The uncaught `require` failure propagates to the per-peer catch in `trickleTick`, which logs "Peer processing failure" and calls `quarantinePeer` — a deterministic LOCAL defect blamed on innocent peers, retrying against the next peer in turn.
- **Fix:** D1: `MAX_PLAINTEXT_BYTES` lowered to **190 * 1024 = 194560** — arithmetic re-verified in python at implementation: base64(194560 + 16) = 259436 chars + 1348 worst-case field-ceiling metadata = 260784 ≤ 262144 (margin 1360); largest sendable plaintext 195581 bytes; 192 KiB demonstrably FAILS (196608 → 262168 b64 before metadata) — do NOT round up. One constant, three call sites inherit it (broadcast gate :859, relay re-encrypt gate :702 — oversized → verbatim relay fallback, Fix 16 behavior preserved; inbound decrypted check :711). The derivation is documented in the constant's comment — the arithmetic IS the fix. D2 (defense in depth): the compress call in `sendToTarget` is caught (`IllegalArgumentException` → log "Frame rejected before send (oversize)" + `return false`, same pattern as the adjacent frameToJson catch) — a future oversize drops locally instead of quarantining a peer; honest caveat: the message stays queued and retries until its 10-min TTL expires (bounded; unreachable after D1). A new `MeshQueue.drop` API was considered and NOT used. Raising `MAX_DECOMPRESSED_BODY_BYTES` REJECTED (wire-protocol change for all peers + widens the deflate-bomb surface).
- **Parent decision (recorded): D1 AS WRITTEN confirmed** — D1 also tightens the inbound check (:711), dropping valid near-maximum reports (plaintext in (194560, 196071] under realistic metadata, a ~1.5 KB window) from old-build peers during transition; accepted cost (ordinary intel is a few hundred bytes). The split-constant alternative (inbound-only ceiling) is NOT taken — one constant, consistent everywhere.
- **Scope judgments:** Fix 16 relay oversized fallback INHERITED (threshold moves, logic untouched; a verbatim relay carries the original ciphertext, already wire-valid, so D1 cannot break relay delivery). Fix 7/7b quarantine mechanics OUT (`quarantinePeer` is correct for genuine peer faults). `MAX_PAYLOAD_B64_LEN` (MeshWire:76) OUT — checked harmless (pre-inflation sanity bound; the real cap is enforced during inflation). Fix 46 follow-ups OUT.
- **Tests: +2 new, 0 updated** (MeshWireTest): `maxAcceptedPlaintextAlwaysFitsTheWire` — worst-case frame at the ceiling (payloadB64 = base64 of `MAX_PLAINTEXT_BYTES + 16` zero bytes, metadata fields at their parse-side ceilings) must compress without throwing; fails on old code by construction (payload 349548 chars → frame ≈ 350896 → compress throws). `oversizeFrameIsRejectedByCompress` — guard test, passes on old code too (say so in the test comment); correction to the prep: one extra payload byte would NOT move the base64 length (quantization — 194576 bytes is 2 mod 3), so the frame carries 2048 extra bytes, which provably crosses the ceiling (payload 262168 chars before metadata). D2 +0 JVM (private service method; review + CI build). Zero `/*` in added Kotlin lines.

- **Fix 56b (CodeRabbit round on PR #187, watch 1 DO NOT MERGE at 2d65415c):** all own checks green (lint-and-test, build-and-test — fourteenth proof new Kotlin tests run green in CI, release, e2e, both Analyze; CodeQL neutral accepted; instrumented in progress = known non-blocker; no Vercel check-run) + CodeRabbit check run success, held by 2 NEW Minors (review id 6052036287), both verified REAL by the parent in code at 2d65415c: (1) Minor 4690544653 (MeshService.kt:111) — the new 190 KiB gate returns false for reports in the 190–256 KiB band, and `broadcastReportToMesh` / `broadcastFailedReportToMesh` in `observatoryMeshBroadcast.ts` discarded the Boolean — a report in that band was dropped without any signal (for the failed-submission path the mesh copy may be the only remaining delivery route); fix = both helpers now check the result, warn on rejection (`console.warn` with the payload length) and return the Boolean to the caller. The caller (`useReportSubmission`) already surfaces the submission error to the user on both failed paths, and the report content survives in the user's offline draft, so no new product behavior is invented — the rejection is no longer silent. Reachability stated honestly: the band needs a ~190 KB description, so ordinary reports (a few hundred bytes) never hit it. (2) Minor 4690544659 (MeshService.kt:1303) — the relay REENCRYPT gate measured DECODED bytes, but `String(bytes, UTF_8)` replaces invalid bytes with U+FFFD (3 bytes each on re-encode), so a decoded payload could pass the gate and still produce a frame over the wire ceiling; `sendToTarget` then dropped it on every tick without recording an attempt — `lastSendAttemptAt` stays 0 and `sweepExpiredMessages` never expires a never-attempted entry (verified: expiry needs `lastSendAttemptAt > 0` or all targets delivered; the queue doc states never-attempted entries "wait indefinitely — bounded by the queue cap"), pinning a queue slot until cap eviction. The D2 comment's "retries until its 10-minute TTL expires" was WRONG for this entry — corrected. Fix = the REENCRYPT branch now checks the re-encoded length via the new pure companion helper `reencodedPlaintextFits(plainBytes)` (JVM-testable, Fix 55 D4 precedent) and falls back to the existing verbatim relay, whose original ciphertext was already wire-valid when received. **+2 Kotlin tests, +4 TS tests** (MeshServiceTrickleTest +2 `invalidUtf8ReencodedPlaintextExceedsTheGate` — 190 KiB of 0xFF passes the decoded gate but re-encodes to 583680 bytes — + `validUtf8AtTheCeilingFitsTheReencodedGate`; new `tests/observatory-mesh-broadcast.test.ts` +4 — both helpers return true on accept and false + warn on reject). The branch wiring is review + CI build. ## Fix 57 — Native navigation, team UI and resources (NACT-1/2, NTEAM-1, NRESBG-1, NRESIC-1)
- **Base:** 6f53aed (Fix 56 merged as PR #187). Triage row 88: 5 findings, all Minor. **NO scope correction: all 5 LIVE at 6f53aed, zero drift** (`git log f1f6557..6f53aed` empty for all touched paths; all anchors re-verified at 6f53aed — stage-2 check :247–250, dialog :217–231, MESH_PERMISSIONS :60–72, sendMeshEcho :190–206, risk colors :366–370, risk_bar background :359, both drawables, RiskScore.labelAr bands :63–71).
- **D1 NACT-1:** pure companion helper `allGranted(results: IntArray)` — ALL requested mesh permissions must be granted; the stage-2 call site used `grantResults[0]` only, so a mixed result with the first granted started MeshService while another transport (e.g. BLUETOOTH_ADVERTISE) was refused and discovery then failed silently. A partial grant now reports the mesh unavailable (behaviour change — PR body). Stage 1 `anyLocationGranted` stays ANY by design (COARSE-only is a valid tier).
- **D2 NACT-2:** the location rationale dialog gains `.setOnCancelListener { permissionStage = 0; requestMeshPermissionsIfNeeded() }` — the exact Later-button continuation. Back/outside cancel previously stalled the ladder and mesh init never started on that activity instance. `setOnDismissListener` rejected (fires after button paths too — would duplicate the request); `setCancelable(false)` rejected (blocks back navigation).
- **D3 NTEAM-1:** one line — `if (ok) chatInput?.setText("")` in `sendMeshEcho`. Failure previously erased the only copy of the typed text while showing "unavailable".
- **D4 NRESBG-1:** `RiskScore.bandColor(score)` is the single source for the 5 band colors (the text-color `when` in ObservatoryFragment now calls it — the 5 literals exist exactly once in the band mapping; other hex occurrences elsewhere are pre-existing brand-color reuse in unrelated chips); the track is a programmatic `GradientDrawable` (LEFT_RIGHT, 99dp corner radius via `TypedValue.applyDimension`) with `bandColor(10/30/50/70/90)` set next to the `riskBar` binding; `bg_risk_track.xml` deleted + its layout reference removed. **Parent deviation from prep:** the prep specified stops at the band centers [0.1, 0.3, 0.5, 0.7, 0.9], but `GradientDrawable` has no public positions API — the 5 colors are evenly distributed (pure at 0/25/50/75/100%). Same colors, same order, only the stop positions differ; the exact-position alternative (a `LinearGradient` shader) needs the laid-out width and a custom drawable — more invasive than the finding warrants for a 14dp backdrop.
- **D5 NRESIC-1:** deleted the unreferenced `ic_hdr_warning.xml` (zero references in `android/` at 6f53aed).
- **Tests: +5 new, +4 updated** (new `NativeMainActivityTest.kt` +5 for `allGranted` — the first-granted/second-denied case fails on old code by construction; `RiskScoreTest.kt` +4 band-color pins: centers + boundaries). NACT-2 / NTEAM-1 / NRESIC-1 are +0 JVM (review + CI build, stated honestly).
- **Follow-up (recorded, NOT fixed here):** the no-fix branch in `sendMeshEcho` calls `broadcastMeshIntel` with null coordinates, which the v2.15.0 honesty gate always rejects — so the `"⚠ بدون تحديد GPS — "` prefix is dead text (never sent, never stored, even locally). Giving coords-less messages a local chat entry (or dropping the branch) is a repository-behaviour decision for a later part.
